   123	/**
   124	 * Capped projection body for a `@cinatra-ai/memory:concept` row (#1379 AC3).
   125	 * Whitelist ONLY: { conceptId, okfType, title (from frontmatter.title), excerpt
   126	 * (bodyMarkdown truncated to 4 KiB) }. NEVER the full envelope — no full
   127	 * bodyMarkdown (64 KiB cap), no raw `frontmatter` passthrough, no `links[]`,
   128	 * no externalId/bundleId. Pure — mirrors `projectArtifactSafe`.
   129	 */
   130	export function projectMemoryConceptCapped(
   131	  data: Record<string, unknown>,
   132	): Record<string, unknown> {
   133	  const conceptId = typeof data.conceptId === "string" ? data.conceptId : undefined;
   134	  const okfType = typeof data.okfType === "string" ? data.okfType : undefined;
   135	  const frontmatter =
   136	    data.frontmatter && typeof data.frontmatter === "object" && !Array.isArray(data.frontmatter)
   137	      ? (data.frontmatter as Record<string, unknown>)
   138	      : undefined;
   139	  const title =
   140	    frontmatter && typeof frontmatter.title === "string" ? frontmatter.title : undefined;
   141	  const bodyMarkdown = typeof data.bodyMarkdown === "string" ? data.bodyMarkdown : "";
   142	  return {
   143	    conceptId,
   144	    okfType,
   145	    title,
   146	    excerpt: truncateToUtf8Bytes(bodyMarkdown, MEMORY_CONCEPT_PROJECTION_EXCERPT_MAX_BYTES),
   147	  };
   148	}
   149	
   150	function deriveEntityName(data: Record<string, unknown>, type: string): string {
   151	  // `frontmatter.title` and `conceptId` are where a memory-concept row keeps its
   152	  // human name — the flat `title` field the earlier candidates look for does not
   153	  // exist on that envelope (cinatra#2591). Without them EVERY memory row derived
   154	  // the bare type id, which was survivable while this only named an episode but
   155	  // is not survivable now that it also names the row's deterministic anchor
   156	  // node: identical names across rows are exactly what graphiti's name-based
   157	  // dedup collapses, and an embedding of the type id ranks for nothing.
   158	  const frontmatter =
   159	    data.frontmatter && typeof data.frontmatter === "object" && !Array.isArray(data.frontmatter)
   160	      ? (data.frontmatter as Record<string, unknown>)
   161	      : undefined;
   162	  const candidate =
   163	    (data.name as string | undefined) ??
   164	    (data.title as string | undefined) ??
   165	    (data.email as string | undefined) ??
   166	    (typeof frontmatter?.title === "string" ? frontmatter.title : undefined) ??
   167	    (typeof data.conceptId === "string" ? data.conceptId : undefined) ??
   168	    type;
   169	  return String(candidate).slice(0, 200);
   170	}
   171	
   172	/**
   173	 * The anchor node's NAME (cinatra#2591).
   174	 *
   175	 * Normally the row's entity name — that is the point: the anchor must rank for
   176	 * the same queries the row should answer, and graphiti embeds node name +
   177	 * summary.
   178	 *
   179	 * The exception is the row that has NO human-meaningful name, where
   180	 * `deriveEntityName` falls through to the bare type id. Seeding many rows under
   181	 * one identical name would push them all into the same dedup bucket and make
   182	 * one anchor stand for many rows. Those rows get a row-scoped suffix instead:
   183	 * still useless for semantic ranking (there is nothing to rank on — that is the
   184	 * input's fault, not the mechanism's), but distinct, so recovery stays
   185	 * one-to-one.
   186	 */
   187	function deriveAnchorName(entityName: string, type: string, objectId: string): string {
   188	  if (entityName !== type) return entityName;
   189	  return `${type} ${objectId.slice(0, 8)}`;
   190	}
   191	
   192	// Metadata/excerpt-only projection for artifact object rows.
   193	// Returns null for non-artifact data (caller keeps the raw-data projection).
   194	// An artifact row is identified by the
   195	// ArtifactObjectData shape (artifactType + latestRepresentationRevisionId).
   196	// Whitelisted fields only; excerpt hard-capped; NEVER body bytes/base64/
   197	// storage keys.
   198	//
   199	// The Graphiti projection carries the semantic identity (the eligible
   200	// extensions + the primary) read from `semantic_assertion`. Callers pass
   201	// the identity via the optional `semanticIdentity` arg; the projector itself
   202	// stays pure (no DB access) so the function remains unit-testable with a
   203	// single fixture call.
   204	const ARTIFACT_EXCERPT_CAP = 2000;
   205	export type ArtifactSemanticIdentity = {
   206	  eligibleExtensions: string[];
   207	  /** The row's primary extension, or NULL when it has no defining extension
   208	   * (epic #1785 — the retired generic catch-all, or no eligible non-floor
   209	   * assertion). */
   210	  primaryExtension: string | null;
   360	    baseType: row.type,
   361	  });
   362	  const identity = enrichment.identity;
   363	  return {
   364	    kind: "faceted",
   365	    body: projectClaimedRowFaceted(row.data, {
   366	      baseType: row.type,
   367	      claimingExtension,
   368	      claimKind: "dedicated",
   369	      claimGeneration: 1,
   370	      effectiveExtension: identity.kind === "extension" ? identity.extension : null,
   371	      eligibleExtensions: enrichment.eligibleExtensions,
   372	    }),
   373	  };
   374	}
   375	
   376	function readCanonicalRow(objectId: string, orgId: string | null): CanonicalRow | null {
   377	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   378	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   379	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   380	    queries: [
   381	      {
   382	        text: `SELECT id, type, data, org_id, version, run_id, agent_id, graphiti_episode_uuid, graphiti_projected_version, source, created_at, owner_level, owner_id, visibility, project_id, projected_group_id
   383	             FROM "${schema}"."objects"
   384	             WHERE id = $1 AND (org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL) AND deleted_at IS NULL
   385	             LIMIT 1`,
   386	        values: [objectId, orgId],
   387	      },
   388	    ],
   389	  });
   390	  return (result?.rows[0] as CanonicalRow | undefined) ?? null;
   391	}
   392	
   393	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   394	// DETERMINISTIC ANCHOR NODE (cinatra#2591 deliverable 4).
   395	//
   396	// THE DEFECT. Recall resolves graph hits back to canonical rows by reading a
   397	// row id off the entity nodes `search_nodes` returns. Four probes did that, and
   398	// three were measured INERT against Graphiti 0.28.2 (live verification
   399	// 2026-04-30): custom episode-body fields do not propagate to node attributes,
   400	// and the `[oid:…]` name tag does not survive extraction. The surviving probe
   401	// fired only when the extraction model INCIDENTALLY emitted the row UUID as an
   402	// entity-node name. So "did recall find this row" depended on model whim, and
   403	// `no_ids_extracted` could be reported for a row that was definitely indexed.
   404	//
   405	// THE MECHANISM. After the episode is handed over, seed ONE entity node whose
   406	// UUID WE choose — a pure function of the row id and the lane. The graph now
   407	// contains a node that names the row by construction, and the inverse map lives
   408	// in Postgres (`objects.graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`). Recall resolves ranked node
   409	// UUIDs through that column; nothing depends on what the model said.
   410	//
   411	// WHY IT ALSO RANKS. The anchor is not an opaque marker: its NAME is the row's
   412	// entity name, which is exactly what graphiti embeds and what
   413	// NODE_HYBRID_SEARCH_RRF ranks on. Measured on the wire 2026-08-10 against real
   414	// Neo4j: a seeded anchor came back from a semantic query that did not contain
   415	// its literal name.
   416	//
   417	// WHY IT NEEDS NO LLM. `add_triplet` writes through graphiti-core's embedder
   418	// path — node-name and fact embeddings — and reaches the LLM only if name-based
   419	// dedup finds an ambiguous candidate. With the local embedder floor
   420	// (docker/kg-embedder) an install with NO extraction provider still seeds and
   421	// still ranks. That is stronger than the issue asked for: ranking now depends
   422	// on the embedder, not on extraction.
   423	//
   424	// IDEMPOTENCE. graphiti-core looks the supplied UUID up first
   425	// (`EntityNode.get_by_uuid`), so re-projecting the same row re-writes the same
   426	// node instead of accumulating duplicates — measured: a second seed left
   427	// exactly one node carrying that name, with the same UUID.
   428	//
   429	// BEST EFFORT, ON PURPOSE. A failed seed must not fail the projection: the
   430	// episode is already handed over, and the row must still be marked projected or
   431	// the outbox would retry forever and re-bill extraction each time. A null
   432	// anchor degrades recall for that row to the legacy probes — the behaviour
   433	// before this change — and says so in the log.
   434	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   435	
   436	/** The lane-scoped anchor node UUID for a row. Pure; distinct namespace from
   437	 *  the episode UUID so the two identities can never collide in the store. */
   438	export function anchorNodeUuidFor(objectId: string, groupId: string): string {
   439	  return identityHashToUuid(`anchor:${objectId}`, groupId);
   440	}
   441	
   442	async function seedAnchorNode(input: {
   443	  objectId: string;
   444	  groupId: string;
   445	  entityName: string;
   446	  type: string;
   447	}): Promise<string | null> {
   448	  const proposed = anchorNodeUuidFor(input.objectId, input.groupId);
   449	  const anchorName = deriveAnchorName(input.entityName, input.type, input.objectId);
   450	  try {
   451	    const result = await addTriplet({
   452	      source_node_name: anchorName,
   453	      edge_name: "RECORDED_IN",
   454	      fact: `${anchorName} is recorded in the cinatra object store.`,
   455	      // One hub node per lane. It is the edge's target only because
   456	      // `add_triplet` writes a TRIPLET; the anchor is the source node, and the
   457	      // hub carries no row identity of its own.
   458	      target_node_name: "cinatra object store",
   459	      group_id: input.groupId,
   460	      source_node_uuid: proposed,
   461	    });
   462	    // Read the uuid BACK. It is normally the one we proposed, but graphiti may
   463	    // resolve a new node onto an existing near-duplicate, and then the resolved
   464	    // uuid is what recall will actually see.
   465	    const resolved = result.nodes.find((n) => n.name === anchorName)?.uuid ?? null;
   466	    if (resolved && resolved !== proposed) {
   467	      console.log(
   468	        `[graphiti-projector] anchor for ${input.objectId} resolved onto an existing node ` +
   469	          `(${proposed} -> ${resolved}); recording the resolved uuid.`,
   470	      );
   471	    }
   472	    return resolved;
   473	  } catch (err) {
   474	    console.warn(
   475	      `[graphiti-projector] anchor seeding failed for ${input.objectId} in lane ${input.groupId} ` +
   476	        "— the episode was still handed over, but recall for this row falls back to " +
   477	        "incidental id extraction until the next projection:",
   478	      err,
   479	    );
   480	    return null;
   481	  }
   482	}
   483	
   484	function markProjected(input: {
   485	  objectId: string;
   486	  episodeUuid: string;
   487	  projectedVersion: number;
   488	  // The lane this projection landed in — persisted so a later scope change can
   489	  // read the OLD lane and delete its episode (#1379 lane-move). Passed for
   490	  // every path (memory rows carry the nested lane; non-memory/adapter rows
   491	  // carry the ambient org lane) so the column is always populated post-project.
   492	  projectedGroupId: string;
   493	  // The deterministic anchor node this row is seeded as, or null when seeding
   494	  // failed. COALESCE below keeps a previously-good anchor rather than clearing
   495	  // it on one transient failure.
   496	  anchorNodeUuid: string | null;
   497	}): void {
   498	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   499	  runPostgresQueriesSync({
   500	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   501	    queries: [
   502	      {
   503	        text: `UPDATE "${schema}"."objects"
   504	             SET graphiti_sync_status = 'synced',
   505	                 graphiti_episode_uuid = $1,
   506	                 graphiti_projected_version = $2,
   507	                 graphiti_projected_at = now(),
   508	                 graphiti_projection_error = NULL,
   509	                 projected_group_id = $4,
   510	                 graphiti_anchor_node_uuid = COALESCE($5, graphiti_anchor_node_uuid)
   511	             WHERE id = $3
   512	               AND (graphiti_projected_version IS NULL OR graphiti_projected_version < $2)`,
   513	        values: [
   514	          input.episodeUuid,
   515	          input.projectedVersion,
   516	          input.objectId,
   517	          input.projectedGroupId,
   518	          input.anchorNodeUuid,
   519	        ],
   520	      },
   521	    ],
   522	  });
   523	  // 0 rows affected ⇒ a newer version already won; benign.
   524	}
   525	
   526	/**
   527	 * Retract a lane-eligible row's lane bookkeeping after its prior-lane episode
   528	 * has been deleted because the row transitioned to a NON-projected scope
   529	 * (visibility='public' or an unclassifiable scope — #1379 memory, #1436
   530	 * artifact). Clears projected_group_id + graphiti_episode_uuid so a re-enqueue
   531	 * does not re-delete an already-gone episode (prior lane reads back as "none"),
   532	 * and records that the row is no longer projected. graphiti_projected_version is
   533	 * intentionally left untouched (a later scope change back to a real lane bumps
   534	 * the version and re-projects fresh). */
   535	function markLaneRetracted(objectId: string): void {
   536	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   537	  runPostgresQueriesSync({
   538	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   539	    queries: [
   540	      {
   541	        text: `UPDATE "${schema}"."objects"
   542	             SET graphiti_sync_status = 'skipped',
   543	                 graphiti_episode_uuid = NULL,
   544	                 projected_group_id = NULL,
   545	                 graphiti_projected_at = now(),
   546	                 graphiti_projection_error = NULL
   547	             WHERE id = $1`,
   548	        values: [objectId],
   549	      },
   550	    ],
   551	  });
   552	}
   553	
   554	/**
   555	 * Retract a row that transitions to a NON-projected state — a lane-derivation
   556	 * terminal skip (public / unclassifiable scope) OR a winning claim disposition
   557	 * of 'none'. Best-effort purge the PRIOR-lane episode (located via the PERSISTED
   558	 * projected_group_id, never re-derived) and clear the lane bookkeeping so a
   559	 * re-enqueue does not re-attempt the purge. A row that was never projected
   560	 * (priorLane null) just logs the skip. Best-effort + swallowed: the purge is
   600	  });
   601	}
   602	
   603	/**
   604	 * Project an object to Graphiti as a NEW append-only episode.
   605	 * Calls addEpisode only; never deleteEpisode + addEpisode.
   606	 *
   607	 * Returns `{ episodeUuid: string }` on success, or `{ episodeUuid: null,
   608	 * skipped: true }` when the canonical row's version has already advanced
   609	 * past this outbox entry (stale-outbox guard).
   610	 */
   611	export async function projectObjectToGraphiti(input: {
   612	  objectId: string;
   613	  objectVersion: number;
   614	  orgId: string | null;
   615	}): Promise<{ episodeUuid: string | null; skipped?: boolean; anchorNodeUuid?: string | null }> {
   616	  // episodeUuid is "" when Graphiti returns no uuid in any known shape.
   617	  // Callers should treat empty string as a soft-failure signal (still marks
   618	  // the row projected to avoid retry loops, but downstream lookups by uuid
   619	  // will return nothing — the reconcile CLI handles such orphans).
   620	  const row = readCanonicalRow(input.objectId, input.orgId);
   621	  if (!row) {
   622	    throw new Error(`projectObjectToGraphiti: canonical row not found id=${input.objectId}`);
   623	  }
   624	  // Stale-outbox guard (BEFORE addEpisode).
   625	  // If the canonical row has already advanced past this outbox entry's
   626	  // object_version, a newer entry will land later and overwrite the episode
   627	  // pointer. Calling addEpisode here would append a stale ghost episode that
   628	  // the version-guard markProjected UPDATE could not later retract. Guard the
   629	  // network call BEFORE making it.
   630	  if (row.version > input.objectVersion) {
   631	    console.log(
   632	      `[graphiti-projector] skipping stale outbox entry for ${input.objectId} ` +
   633	        `(row.version=${row.version} > input.objectVersion=${input.objectVersion})`,
   634	    );
   635	    return { episodeUuid: null, skipped: true };
   636	  }
   637	
   638	  // Equal-version dedup guard (BEFORE addEpisode, BEFORE adapter routing).
   639	  // The stale guard above only rejects entries whose CANONICAL version has
   640	  // advanced, and markProjected only moves graphiti_projected_version on
   641	  // strictly newer projections — so a re-enqueued outbox item for a version
   642	  // that is already projected (equal or older than graphiti_projected_version)
   643	  // would otherwise call addEpisode again and append a duplicate episode.
   644	  // Skip terminally instead: the outbox row settles as done (not failed),
   645	  // no retry, zero new episodes. `!= null` (loose) covers both SQL NULL and
   646	  // an absent field.
   647	  if (
   648	    row.graphiti_projected_version != null &&
   649	    input.objectVersion <= row.graphiti_projected_version
   650	  ) {
   651	    console.log(
   652	      `[graphiti-projector] skipping already-projected outbox entry for ${input.objectId} ` +
   653	        `(objectVersion=${input.objectVersion} <= graphiti_projected_version=${row.graphiti_projected_version})`,
   654	    );
   655	    return { episodeUuid: null, skipped: true };
   656	  }
   657	
   658	  // Source gate. Graphiti indexes data ORIGINATED in cinatra: agent writes,
   659	  // UI writes, and authenticated HTTP-route writes (canonical artifact
   660	  // creation — uploads included — persists source='route'; see the objects
   661	  // INSERT in src/lib/artifacts/artifact-creation.ts). Data PULLED from
   662	  // external systems by background sync (worker | scheduler) is NOT
   663	  // projected. Rows with source ∉ {agent, ui, route} are skipped terminally —
   664	  // the outbox row is marked done (not failed), no retry. A `null` source
   665	  // predates this gate and is treated as cinatra-originated to avoid
   666	  // dropping legacy rows.
   667	  if (
   668	    row.source !== null &&
   669	    row.source !== "agent" &&
   670	    row.source !== "ui" &&
   671	    row.source !== "route"
   672	  ) {
   673	    console.log(
   674	      `[graphiti-projector] skipping non-cinatra-originated row ${row.id} (source=${row.source})`,
   675	    );
   676	    return { episodeUuid: null, skipped: true };
   677	  }
   678	
   679	  // Adapter routing. Adapter-owned types (CRM account/contact under
   680	  // the Twenty migration) route here to TwentyToGraphitiAdapter.export(),
   681	  // which hydrates the full record from Twenty via the crm_* facade before
   682	  // composing the episode. Filter to targetSystem === "graphiti" so a
   683	  // future non-Graphiti sync adapter on the same type cannot be invoked by
   684	  // this Graphiti-specific projection path. The adapter calls addEpisode
   685	  // itself + returns a deterministic externalId (the episode UUID) which we
   686	  // use for markProjected bookkeeping.
   687	  const adapters = objectSyncAdapterRegistry
   688	    .getAdaptersForType(row.type)
   689	    .filter((a: ObjectSyncAdapter) => a.targetSystem === "graphiti");
   690	  if (adapters.length > 0) {
   691	    const adapter = adapters[0]!;
   692	    const storedObject: StoredObject = {
   693	      id: row.id,
   694	      type: row.type,
   695	      data: row.data,
   696	      parentId: null,
   697	      orgId: row.org_id,
   698	      createdAt: row.created_at,
   699	      createdBy: null,
   700	      agentId: row.agent_id,
   701	      runId: row.run_id,
   702	      source: (row.source as StoredObject["source"]) ?? null,
   703	      classificationConfidence: null,
   704	      exportedTo: {},
   705	      deletedAt: null,
   706	    };
   707	    // Adapters are configless built-ins for now (the Twenty→Graphiti
   708	    // adapter ships without a config row). Pass an empty config; future per-row
   709	    // configs can plug in via `readActiveObjectSyncAdapterConfigs` here.
   710	    const result = await adapter.export(storedObject, {} as never);
   711	    if (!result.ok) {
   712	      // Adapter export failed — let processProjectionOutbox surface this as
   713	      // a failed outbox row (same retry semantics as the generic path).
   714	      throw new Error(
   715	        `adapter ${adapter.id} export failed for ${row.id}: ${result.error ?? "(no error message)"}`,
   716	      );
   717	    }
   718	    // externalId is the deterministic episode UUID the adapter generated +
   719	    // recorded with Graphiti. Use it for the projector's bookkeeping so
   720	    // future delete attempts hit the right episode.
   721	    const adapterEpisodeUuid = result.externalId ?? "";
   722	    markProjected({
   723	      objectId: row.id,
   724	      episodeUuid: adapterEpisodeUuid,
   725	      projectedVersion: input.objectVersion,
   726	      // Adapter-owned types (CRM pointer rows) are never memory-scoped and
   727	      // project to the ambient org lane.
   728	      projectedGroupId: deriveProjectionGroupId(input.orgId ?? row.org_id),
   729	      // Adapter-owned rows (CRM pointer rows) are projected by the adapter's own
   730	      // episode, not by the body this file builds, so there is no anchor to
   731	      // seed here. COALESCE in markProjected leaves any prior anchor intact.
   732	      anchorNodeUuid: null,
   733	    });
   734	    return { episodeUuid: adapterEpisodeUuid };
   735	  }
   736	
   737	  // Claimed typed rows leave the raw path (#1427 AC-3). Resolved AFTER
   738	  // adapter routing on purpose: adapter-owned types (CRM pointer rows) are
   739	  // never claimed in this epic (#1424 guardrail) and keep adapter ownership
   740	  // regardless. Generic artifact rows are never claimed either — they keep
   741	  // the projectArtifactSafe path below.
   742	  const claimedProjection =
   743	    row.org_id !== null && row.type !== GENERIC_ARTIFACT_OBJECT_TYPE
   744	      ? resolveClaimedRowProjection(row)
   745	      : null;
   746	  if (claimedProjection?.kind === "skip") {
   747	    // The winning claim's dispositions say projection: 'none' — terminal skip:
   748	    // the outbox row settles done, nothing is projected, no retry. If the row
   749	    // was PREVIOUSLY projected (e.g. an artifact-safe -> none disposition flip
   750	    // that left a nested-lane episode), best-effort purge that prior-lane
   751	    // episode + clear the bookkeeping so it is not orphaned (#1436) — the
   752	    // ambient-group rebuild would never reach a nested lane.
   753	    await retractPriorLaneEpisode(
   754	      row,
   755	      "winning claim dispositions set projection='none'",
   756	    );
   757	    return { episodeUuid: null, skipped: true };
   758	  }
   759	
   760	  // Group-id (Graphiti lane) derivation. LANE-ELIGIBLE rows NEST under the org
   761	  // lane via their server-derived scope: memory concepts (#1379 AC1) UNION
   762	  // generic artifact rows UNION claimed rows whose winning disposition is
   763	  // artifact-safe / faceted (#1436 AC1). A claimed 'raw' explicit opt-in,
   764	  // adapter-owned CRM rows (routed to the adapter above and never reaching
   765	  // here), and every other typed row keep the SINGLE ambient org lane (epic
   766	  // #1424 guardrail — deriveProjectionGroupId stays single-lane-per-org for
   767	  // those). All lane-eligible classes reuse the SAME hardened scope->lane
   768	  // function (deriveScopeLane) — no independent artifact branch that could
   769	  // reintroduce the #1379 phantom-team-lane bug (#1436 AC6).
   770	  const isMemory = row.type === MEMORY_CONCEPT_TYPE_ID;
   771	  const isLaneEligible =
   772	    isMemory ||
   773	    row.type === GENERIC_ARTIFACT_OBJECT_TYPE ||
   774	    claimedProjection?.kind === "faceted";
   775	  // The prior lane is the PERSISTED `projected_group_id` (NOT re-derived from
   776	  // the current scope — that already reflects the NEW scope). The lane-scoped
   777	  // deterministic UUID (identityHashToUuid(row.id, priorLane)) locates the
   778	  // prior-lane episode for the best-effort purge below.
   779	  //
   780	  // SECURITY BOUNDARY NOTE. The Graphiti lane is a RECALL-RELEVANCE scoping
   781	  // mechanism, NOT the authorization boundary. The authz boundary is the
   782	  // recall handler's canonical re-fetch: objects_list resolves searchNodes
   783	  // candidate ids, then `listObjectsByFilter(..., scopeActor)` re-reads the
   784	  // Postgres rows through `buildOwnershipFilter` (owner_level/owner_id/
   785	  // visibility) and `enforceResourceAccess(object.read)` re-checks each — on
   786	  // the CURRENT scope. So a stale/orphaned episode in an old lane can at worst
   787	  // surface a candidate id that the Postgres ownership filter then DROPS; it
   788	  // can never disclose content under a scope the actor no longer has. The
   789	  // episode purge here is therefore relevance HYGIENE, and it is best-effort:
   790	  // Graphiti gives no episode-uuid control (see graphiti-rebuild.ts — the
   791	  // authoritative purge is a group rebuild, not per-episode delete), exactly
   792	  // like `deleteCurrentEpisodeFromGraphiti`. A delete failure must NOT block
   793	  // re-projection (that would strand the row out of its new lane), so it is
   794	  // swallowed + warned, mirroring the object-delete path.
   795	  const priorLane = row.projected_group_id;
   796	  let groupId: string;
   797	  if (isLaneEligible) {
   798	    const derivation = deriveScopeLane(input.orgId ?? row.org_id, {
   799	      ownerLevel: row.owner_level,
   800	      ownerId: row.owner_id,
   801	      visibility: row.visibility,
   802	      projectId: row.project_id,
   803	    });
   804	    if (derivation.kind === "skip") {
   805	      // Terminal skip (public / unclassifiable scope). Best-effort purge any
   806	      // prior-lane episode + clear the bookkeeping (shared with the claim-'none'
   807	      // skip). Recall authz is enforced Postgres-side on the current scope
   808	      // regardless.
   809	      await retractPriorLaneEpisode(row, derivation.reason);
   810	      return { episodeUuid: null, skipped: true };
   811	    }
   812	    groupId = derivation.groupId;
   813	  } else {
   814	    groupId = deriveProjectionGroupId(input.orgId ?? row.org_id);
   815	  }
   816	  // Lane-move (#1379 AC2, generalized to artifact rows #1436 AC2). When the prior
   817	  // lane differs from the newly-derived lane — a scope change between nested
   818	  // lanes, OR a claimed row leaving lane treatment entirely because its winning
   819	  // disposition flipped artifact-safe -> raw (nested lane -> the ambient org
   820	  // lane) — best-effort purge the prior-lane episode (its lane-scoped UUID
   821	  // differs from the new one) before projecting into the new lane. Applies to
   822	  // BOTH branches so a lane-eligibility EXIT never strands a nested episode.
   823	  // Best-effort + swallowed for the same reason as the retract above — the purge
   824	  // is relevance hygiene, not the authz gate. A first-ever projection (priorLane
   825	  // null) and a same-lane re-projection (priorLane === groupId) purge nothing.
   826	  if (priorLane && priorLane !== groupId) {
   827	    try {
   828	      await deleteEpisode({ uuid: identityHashToUuid(row.id, priorLane) });
   829	      console.log(
   830	        `[graphiti-projector] lane-move for ${row.id} (${row.type}): purged prior-lane episode ` +
   831	          `(${priorLane} -> ${groupId})`,
   832	      );
   833	    } catch (err) {
   834	      console.warn(
   835	        `[graphiti-projector] lane-move: deleteEpisode failed for prior lane ${priorLane} of ${row.id}:`,
   836	        err,
   837	      );
   838	    }
   839	  }
   840	  // EPISODE-UUID-EMPTY: knowledge-graph-mcp 1.0.x add_memory returns only a
   841	  // message string — no uuid in any known response path. We compute a stable
   842	  // deterministic UUID locally for Postgres bookkeeping and delete attempts.
   843	  // NOTE: do NOT pass uuid to addEpisode. Graphiti 0.28.2 queue_service
   844	  // interprets the uuid param as "re-process an existing node" — it issues
   845	  // MATCH (uuid) which fails with "node not found" when the episode is new,
   846	  // permanently blocking its processing. Episodes must be created without uuid.
   847	  //
   848	  // Embed [oid:<objectId>] in the episode name so it travels with
   849	  // the episode record. NOTE: live verification (2026-04-30) showed this tag does
   850	  // NOT propagate to entity node names via LLM extraction, and re-checking on
   851	  // the replacement server (cinatra#2591) did not change that — it is a property
   852	  // of extraction, not of the wrapper. So OID_RE in handlers.ts stays inert; it
   853	  // survives only as one of the demoted incidental probes. Row recovery is now
   854	  // carried by the deterministic anchor node seeded below, not by this tag.
   855	  const episodeUuid = identityHashToUuid(row.id, groupId);
   856	  // Artifact projection policy. Artifact rows MUST NOT
   857	  // spread raw `row.data` into graph memory: even though the artifact writer
   858	  // keeps bytes/base64/storage keys OUT of objects.data by invariant
   859	  // by invariant, defence-in-depth requires a metadata/excerpt-only
   860	  // projection so a future writer bug, a large editable body, or stray
   861	  // fields can never poison Graphiti. Non-artifact rows are unchanged.
   862	  //
   863	  // For semantic artifact rows, fetch the eligible semantic assertions
   864	  // in lock-step with the projection so Graphiti sees the current
   865	  // `primaryExtension` + the eligible extension set. Reads
   866	  // `semantic_assertion` directly (this file already has PG access).
   867	  // Skips for non-artifact rows (no extra query) and for rows with no
   868	  // semantic_assertion entries, which naturally return empty arrays.
   869	  // Skip the assertion lookup when org_id is null (project / no-tenant
   870	  // path — semantic_assertion is org-scoped, would return nothing
   871	  // anyway, so the resulting empty identity is identical).
   872	  const semanticIdentity =
   873	    row.type === "@cinatra-ai/artifact:object" && row.org_id !== null
   874	      ? readSemanticIdentityForProjection(row.org_id, row.id)
   875	      : undefined;
   876	  // Precedence: claimed faceted body > claimed explicit-raw opt-in >
   877	  // memory-concept capped body (#1379 AC3) > generic-artifact safe projection >
   878	  // raw data (unclaimed non-artifact rows — unchanged pre-#1427 behavior).
   879	  // Memory rows are never claimed (claims are artifact-type claims), so the
   880	  // capped branch sits just after the claim branches: it replaces the raw-data
   881	  // default those rows previously fell through to (which leaked the full 64 KiB
   882	  // envelope — frontmatter, links, bundleId).
   883	  const projectionData =
   884	    claimedProjection?.kind === "faceted"
   885	      ? claimedProjection.body
   886	      : claimedProjection?.kind === "raw"
   887	        ? row.data
   888	        : isMemory
   889	          ? projectMemoryConceptCapped(row.data)
   890	          : (projectArtifactSafe(row.data, semanticIdentity) ?? row.data);
   891	  const episodeBody = JSON.stringify({
   892	    ...projectionData,
   893	    cinatra_object_id: row.id,
   894	    _cinatra: {
   895	      objectId: row.id,
   896	      version: row.version,
   897	      type: row.type,
   898	      runId: row.run_id,
   899	      agentId: row.agent_id,
   900	    },
   901	  });
   902	
   903	  const entityName = deriveEntityName(row.data, row.type);
   904	
   905	  await addEpisode({
   906	    name: `${entityName} [oid:${row.id}]`,
   907	    episode_body: episodeBody,
   908	    source: "json",
   909	    source_description: `objects projection (run ${row.run_id ?? "n/a"})`,
   910	    group_id: groupId,
   911	    // uuid intentionally omitted — see EPISODE-UUID-EMPTY note above
   912	  });
   913	
   914	  const anchorNodeUuid = await seedAnchorNode({
   915	    objectId: row.id,
   916	    groupId,
   917	    entityName,
   918	    type: row.type,
   919	  });
   920	
   921	  markProjected({
   922	    objectId: row.id,
   923	    episodeUuid,
   924	    projectedVersion: input.objectVersion,
   925	    // Persist the lane just projected into so a later scope change can locate
   926	    // and delete THIS episode (#1379 lane-move bookkeeping).
   927	    projectedGroupId: groupId,
   928	    anchorNodeUuid,
   929	  });
   930	  return { episodeUuid, anchorNodeUuid };
   931	}
   932	
   933	// Caller note:
   934	// `processProjectionOutbox` must treat `{ skipped: true }` as a successful
   935	// outcome and mark the corresponding outbox row `done` (not `failed`). The
   936	// loop body in this file already wraps the call in try/catch — the success
   937	// path falls through to the "Mark outbox row done" UPDATE, which is the
   938	// correct behavior for skipped entries (do not retry; a newer outbox row
   939	// will deliver the latest state). No special-casing needed in the worker
   940	// loop, but executors MUST verify the resolved value is awaited (not the
   941	// promise) so the skipped flag is observable.
   942	
   943	/**
   944	 * Delete the current episode pointer (does NOT hard-delete extracted entities).
   945	 */
   946	export async function deleteCurrentEpisodeFromGraphiti(input: {
   947	  objectId: string;
   948	  orgId: string | null;
   949	}): Promise<void> {
   950	  const row = readCanonicalRow(input.objectId, input.orgId);
   951	  if (!row) return; // already gone
   952	  if (row.graphiti_episode_uuid) {
   953	    try {
   954	      await deleteEpisode({ uuid: row.graphiti_episode_uuid });
   955	    } catch (err) {
   956	      console.warn(
   957	        `[graphiti-projector] deleteEpisode failed for ${input.objectId}:`,
   958	        err,
   959	      );
   960	      // Continue — we still mark deleted in PG; the cleanup CLI handles orphans.
   560	 * appending each value to `values` at the caller's running parameter index.
   561	 * Defense-in-depth against SQL injection through the interpolated key:
   562	 *   1. the key must be in DATA_EQUALS_ALLOWED_KEYS (closed set), AND
   563	 *   2. it must match a strict identifier pattern (no quotes / whitespace).
   564	 * A key failing either check THROWS — a mis-typed correlation filter is a
   565	 * programming error, never a silently-dropped clause that would widen the read.
   566	 */
   567	export function compileDataEqualsClause(
   568	  dataEquals: ReadonlyArray<{ key: string; value: string }> | undefined,
   569	  values: unknown[],
   570	  startIndex: number,
   571	): { clauses: string[]; nextIndex: number } {
   572	  const clauses: string[] = [];
   573	  let pIdx = startIndex;
   574	  if (!dataEquals || dataEquals.length === 0) return { clauses, nextIndex: pIdx };
   575	  for (const { key, value } of dataEquals) {
   576	    if (!DATA_EQUALS_ALLOWED_KEYS.has(key) || !DATA_EQUALS_KEY_PATTERN.test(key)) {
   577	      throw new Error(
   578	        `listObjectsByFilter: dataEquals key "${key}" is not an allow-listed indexed correlation key ` +
   579	          `(${[...DATA_EQUALS_ALLOWED_KEYS].join(", ")})`,
   580	      );
   581	    }
   582	    clauses.push(`data->>'${key}' = $${pIdx}`);
   583	    values.push(value);
   584	    pIdx += 1;
   585	  }
   586	  return { clauses, nextIndex: pIdx };
   587	}
   588	
   589	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   590	// Deterministic graph-node -> canonical-row resolution (cinatra#2591).
   591	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   592	//
   593	// `search_nodes` ranks and returns entity-node UUIDs. This turns them back into
   594	// canonical object ids by reading the DETERMINISTIC anchor node each projected
   595	// row is seeded as (`graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`, written by the projector).
   596	//
   597	// It replaces a chain of four probes that read an id off whatever the
   598	// extraction model happened to emit — three of which were already measured
   599	// inert against the graph, and the fourth of which fired only when the model
   600	// incidentally produced the row UUID as an entity name. That chain is why
   601	// recall could answer `no_ids_extracted` for a row that was definitely indexed.
   602	//
   603	// AUTHORIZATION IS DELIBERATELY NOT HERE. This is id resolution only: it takes
   604	// UUIDs the graph already returned and says which rows they name. Every
   605	// resolved id is then re-fetched through `listObjectsByFilter` with the caller's
   606	// actor, which applies the ownership filter, the sealed-room project clause and
   607	// the soft-delete filter in SQL. That is the same shape the Graphiti-extracted
   608	// ids already flowed through, so the read boundary is unchanged — this only
   609	// makes the candidate set correct.
   610	//
   611	// Returns a Map so the caller can preserve the graph's RANK order (a plain
   612	// array of rows would lose it; `rows.find()` per uuid would be O(n^2)).
   613	export function resolveObjectIdsByAnchorNodeUuids(
   614	  nodeUuids: readonly string[],
   615	  orgId: string | null,
   616	): Map<string, string> {
   617	  const resolved = new Map<string, string>();
   618	  const unique = [...new Set(nodeUuids.filter((u) => typeof u === "string" && u.length > 0))];
   619	  if (unique.length === 0) return resolved;
   620	
   621	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   622	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   623	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   624	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   625	    queries: [
   626	      {
   627	        // `(org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL)` mirrors listObjectsByFilter's own org
   628	        // clause, so an org-scoped recall can never resolve a UUID onto another
   629	        // tenant's row even before the actor filter runs.
   630	        text: `SELECT id, graphiti_anchor_node_uuid
   631	FROM "${schema}"."objects"
   632	WHERE graphiti_anchor_node_uuid = ANY($1::text[])
   633	  AND (org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL)
   634	  AND deleted_at IS NULL`,
   635	        values: [unique, orgId],
   636	      },
   637	    ],
   638	  });
   639	
   640	  for (const row of result?.rows ?? []) {
   641	    const nodeUuid = (row as { graphiti_anchor_node_uuid?: unknown }).graphiti_anchor_node_uuid;
   642	    const id = (row as { id?: unknown }).id;
   643	    if (typeof nodeUuid === "string" && typeof id === "string") resolved.set(nodeUuid, id);
   644	  }
   645	  return resolved;
   646	}
   647	
   648	export function listObjectsByFilter(
   649	  filter: ListObjectsFilter,
   650	  actor?: ActorContext,
   651	): ObjectRecord[] {
   652	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   653	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   654	
   655	  const where: string[] = [`(org_id = $1 OR $1 IS NULL)`, `deleted_at IS NULL`];
   656	  const values: unknown[] = [filter.orgId];
   657	  let pIdx = 2;
   658	
   659	  if (filter.type) {
   660	    where.push(`type = $${pIdx}`);
   661	    values.push(filter.type);
   662	    pIdx += 1;
   663	  }
   664	  if (filter.runId) {
   665	    where.push(`run_id = $${pIdx}`);
   666	    values.push(filter.runId);
   667	    pIdx += 1;
   668	  }
   669	  if (filter.ids && filter.ids.length > 0) {
   670	    where.push(`id = ANY($${pIdx}::text[])`);
   671	    values.push(filter.ids);
   672	    pIdx += 1;
   673	  }
   674	
   675	  // sealedRoomFilterValue() returns the effective projectId, or null when
   676	  // ambient or when the per-table feature flag is OFF. When non-null, append
   677	  // `AND project_id = $projectId` so the result is intersected with the
   678	  // project boundary. This clause runs before the per-actor ownership filter
   679	  // and after the `id IN (...)` clause, so a Graphiti candidate set from
   680	  // P+Q+ambient is filtered down to P-only. The decision lives here in the
   681	  // data layer; handlers can't bypass it.
   682	  const effectiveProjectId = sealedRoomFilterValue("objects", filter.projectId);
   683	  if (effectiveProjectId !== null) {
   684	    where.push(`project_id = $${pIdx}`);
   685	    values.push(effectiveProjectId);
   686	    pIdx += 1;
   687	  }
   688	
   689	  // cinatra#1456: indexed `data->>'<key>' = $n` correlation filters. Runs
   690	  // BEFORE the per-actor ownership filter so it intersects with org/type/
   691	  // sealed-room and cannot widen the read. Keys are allow-listed + pattern-
   692	  // validated in compileDataEqualsClause (the interpolated-key injection guard).
   693	  {
   694	    const { clauses, nextIndex } = compileDataEqualsClause(filter.dataEquals, values, pIdx);
   695	    for (const clause of clauses) where.push(clause);
   696	    pIdx = nextIndex;
   697	  }
   698	
   699	  // Splice buildOwnershipFilter into WHERE when actor present.
   700	  if (actor) {
   700	  if (actor) {
   701	    const frag = buildOwnershipFilter(actor);
   702	    const remapped = frag.sql.replace(/\$(\d+)/g, (_m, n) => `$${Number(n) + pIdx - 1}`);
   703	    where.push(remapped);
   704	    values.push(...frag.params);
   705	    pIdx += frag.params.length;
   706	  }
   707	
   708	  const orderBy =
   709	    filter.ids && filter.ids.length > 0
   710	      ? "" // caller preserves Graphiti rank
   711	      : `ORDER BY created_at DESC`;
   712	  const limitClause = filter.limit
   713	    ? `LIMIT ${Math.min(filter.limit, 1000)}`
   714	    : "LIMIT 100";
   715	
   716	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   717	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   718	    queries: [
   719	      {
   720	        text: `SELECT id, type, parent_id, parent_type, data, created_at, updated_at,
   721	                 created_by, org_id, source, run_id, agent_id, package_version,
   722	                 agent_spec_version, version, deleted_at,
   723	                 owner_level, owner_id, visibility, project_id
   724	               FROM "${schema}"."objects"
   725	               WHERE ${where.join(" AND ")}
   726	               ${orderBy}
   727	               ${limitClause}`,
   728	        values,
   729	      },
   730	    ],
   731	  });
   732	
   733	  return (result?.rows ?? []).map(rowToObjectRecord);
   734	}
   735	
   736	/**
   737	 * Soft-delete: sets `deleted_at` and atomically appends a 'delete' outbox
   738	 * row. NEVER split into two `runPostgresQueriesSync` calls.
   739	 *
   740	 * Conditional outbox via CTE: the CTE below bumps `version`, flips
   741	 * `deleted_at`, and emits the outbox row in the same statement, only when a
   742	 * row actually transitioned. This preserves the atomic-outbox guarantee while
   743	 * eliminating spurious outbox rows for wrong-org or already-deleted updates.
   744	 */
   745	export function softDeleteObject(
   746	  id: string,
   747	  scope: { orgId: string | null },
   748	): { changeSetId: string | null } {
   749	  const { query } = buildSoftDeleteObjectQuery({ id, orgId: scope.orgId });
   750	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   751	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   752	    transaction: true,
   753	    queries: [query],
   754	  });
   755	  return { changeSetId: readSoftDeleteChangeSetId(result) };
   756	}
   757	
   758	/**
   759	 * Read the change_set id surfaced by a `buildSoftDeleteObjectQuery`
   760	 * statement result. NULL when the delete was a no-op (wrong org /
   761	 * already-deleted) — never an Undo deep-link to a change_set that was not
   762	 * created.
   763	 */
   764	export function readSoftDeleteChangeSetId(
   765	  result: { rows: Array<Record<string, unknown>> } | undefined,
   766	): string | null {
   767	  return (result?.rows?.[0]?.change_set_id as string | null | undefined) ?? null;
   768	}
   769	
   770	/**
   771	 * Build the CANONICAL soft-delete statement: one atomic CTE that flips
   772	 * `deleted_at`, bumps `version`, enqueues the Graphiti 'delete' outbox row,
   773	 * and appends the object_change_event + auto-closed restorable change_set
   774	 * (undo eligibility). This is the ONE soft-delete shape — `softDeleteObject`
   775	 * executes it standalone, and the artifact tombstone path (cinatra#1428
   776	 * deletion unification) composes it into the SAME transaction as its
   777	 * retention + provider-cache companions so an artifact delete can never
   778	 * bypass object history or the outbox again.
   779	 *
   780	 * The statement's final SELECT returns `change_set_id` (NULL on a no-op);
   781	 * read it with `readSoftDeleteChangeSetId`.
   782	 *
   783	 * `actorId`/`actorKind` attribute the change event + change_set; they
   784	 * default to the legacy system attribution when omitted.
   785	 */
   786	export function buildSoftDeleteObjectQuery(input: {
   787	  id: string;
   788	  orgId: string | null;
   789	  actorId?: string | null;
   790	  actorKind?: "user" | "agent" | "system";
   791	}): { query: { text: string; values: unknown[] }; changeSetId: string } {
   792	  assertWriteScopeAllowed("softDeleteObject", input.orgId);
   793	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   794	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   795	
   796	  // Emit object_change_event in the same atomic CTE so legacy soft-delete
   797	  // callers participate in the data-safety history substrate.
   798	  const legacyChangeSetId = `cs_legacy_${randomUUID()}`;
   799	  const legacyEventId = randomUUID();
   800	  const legacyIdempotencyKey = `che_legacy_${randomUUID()}`;
   801	  const legacyChecksum = createHash("sha256")
   802	    .update(`legacy-writer:softDeleteObject:${input.id}:${legacyIdempotencyKey}`)
   803	    .digest("hex");
   804	
   805	  const query = {
   806	    text: `WITH base_row AS (
   807	                 SELECT version, row_to_json(o.*)::jsonb AS payload, type, org_id,
   808	                        project_id, owner_level, owner_id, visibility
   809	                 FROM "${schema}"."objects" o WHERE id = $1
   810	               ),
   811	               deleted AS (
   812	                 UPDATE "${schema}"."objects"
   813	                 SET deleted_at = now(),
   814	                     graphiti_sync_status = 'pending',
   815	                     version = COALESCE(version, 0) + 1,
   816	                     updated_at = now()
   817	                 WHERE id = $1
   818	                   AND (org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL)
   819	                   AND deleted_at IS NULL
   820	                 RETURNING id, version AS object_version, org_id, type, project_id,
   821	                           owner_level, owner_id, visibility,
   822	                           row_to_json("${schema}"."objects".*)::jsonb AS row_json
   823	               ),
   824	               outbox_row AS (
   825	                 INSERT INTO "${schema}"."graphiti_projection_outbox"
   826	                   (id, object_id, object_version, org_id, operation, payload_hash, status, attempts)
   827	                 SELECT gen_random_uuid()::text,
   828	                        deleted.id, deleted.object_version, deleted.org_id,
   829	                        'delete', NULL, 'pending', 0
   830	                 FROM deleted
   831	               ),
   832	               new_changeset AS (
   833	                 INSERT INTO "${schema}"."change_set"
   834	                   (id, org_id, opened_at, actor_id, actor_kind, run_id,
   835	                    effect_rollup, restorable, closed_at, closure_reason,
   836	                    created_by, created_at, updated_at)
   837	                 SELECT $3, deleted.org_id, now(), $7, $8, NULL,
   838	                        'reversible-internal', true, now(), 'legacy-writer-auto-close',
   839	                        NULL, now(), now()
   840	                 FROM deleted
   841	                 RETURNING id
   842	               ),
   843	               event_row AS (
   844	                 INSERT INTO "${schema}"."object_change_event"
   845	                   (id, change_set_id, sequence, object_id, object_type, operation,
   846	                    history_effect, before_snapshot, after_snapshot,
   847	                    base_version, result_version, object_schema_version,
   848	                    restore_eligible, restore_ineligible_reason,
   849	                    compensating_template_id, remote_revision_ref,
   850	                    actor_id, actor_kind, run_id, audit_event_id,
   851	                    org_id, project_id, owner_level, owner_id, visibility,
   852	                    idempotency_key, event_checksum, created_at)
   853	                 SELECT $4, $3, 1, deleted.id, deleted.type, 'soft-delete',
   854	                        'reversible-internal',
   855	                        (SELECT payload FROM base_row), deleted.row_json,
   856	                        (SELECT version FROM base_row),
   857	                        deleted.object_version, 'v1',
   858	                        true, NULL, NULL, NULL,
   859	                        $7, $8, NULL, NULL,
   860	                        deleted.org_id, deleted.project_id, deleted.owner_level,
   430	    type: row.type,
   431	    name,
   432	    data,
   433	    classificationConfidence:
   434	      typeof confidenceRaw === "number" ? confidenceRaw : null,
   435	    parentId: row.parentId,
   436	    parentType: row.parentType,
   437	    actor: {
   438	      agentId: row.agentId,
   439	      packageVersion: row.packageVersion,
   440	      agentSpecVersion: row.agentSpecVersion,
   441	      runId: row.runId,
   442	      source: row.source,
   443	      userId: row.createdBy,
   444	    },
   445	    deletedAt: row.deletedAt,
   446	    createdAt: row.createdAt,
   447	    updatedAt: row.updatedAt,
   448	  };
   449	}
   450	
   451	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   452	// Graphiti search_nodes -> objectId extraction
   453	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   454	//
   455	// DETERMINISTIC FIRST, incidental second (cinatra#2591 deliverable 4).
   456	//
   457	// WHAT THIS USED TO BE. A chain of four probes that read a row id off whatever
   458	// Graphiti's extraction model happened to put on an entity node. Three of them
   459	// were measured INERT against Graphiti 0.28.2 (live verification 2026-04-30 —
   460	// custom episode-body fields do not reach node attributes, and the `[oid:…]`
   461	// name tag does not survive extraction). The fourth fired only when the model
   462	// incidentally emitted the row UUID as an entity-node name. So a recall could
   463	// report `no_ids_extracted` for a row that was definitely indexed, and the
   464	// planned `memory_recall` semantic path (cinatra#1380) would have inherited
   465	// that.
   466	//
   467	// WHAT IT IS NOW. Every projected row is seeded as a DETERMINISTIC anchor node
   468	// whose UUID is a pure function of the row id and the lane
   469	// (`graphiti-projector.ts` -> `anchorNodeUuidFor`), and the inverse map lives in
   470	// Postgres (`objects.graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`). Ranked node UUIDs resolve
   471	// through that column. Nothing depends on model whim.
   472	//
   473	// THE LEGACY PROBES ARE KEPT, DEMOTED. They are the ONLY recovery path for rows
   474	// projected before this change (their anchor column is null until the next
   475	// projection) and for adapter-owned rows that project through their own
   476	// episode. They run second and only add ids the deterministic pass did not
   477	// already produce. They are not the mechanism; they are the tail.
   478	const OID_RE = /\[oid:([0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12})\]/;
   479	const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
   480	
   481	/** The pre-#2591 chain: ids read off model-produced node fields. Inert for most
   482	 *  nodes by measurement; retained for rows with no anchor yet. */
   483	function extractIncidentalObjectIds(nodes: EntityNode[]): string[] {
   484	  const ids: string[] = [];
   485	  for (const n of nodes) {
   486	    const attrs = (n as unknown as { attributes?: Record<string, unknown> })
   487	      .attributes;
   488	    const labels = (n as unknown as { labels?: string[] }).labels;
   489	    const candidate =
   490	      (attrs?.cinatra_object_id as unknown) ??
   491	      (n as unknown as { cinatra_object_id?: unknown }).cinatra_object_id ??
   492	      OID_RE.exec(n.name)?.[1] ??
   493	      (labels?.includes("Object") && UUID_RE.test(n.name ?? "") ? n.name : undefined);
   494	    if (typeof candidate === "string" && candidate.length > 0) {
   495	      ids.push(candidate);
   496	    }
   497	  }
   498	  return ids;
   499	}
   500	
   501	/**
   502	 * Ranked entity nodes -> canonical object ids, RANK PRESERVED.
   503	 *
   504	 * Walks the nodes in the order Graphiti ranked them and emits, per node, the
   505	 * deterministically-anchored row id when there is one, otherwise whatever the
   506	 * incidental probes can recover. De-duplicated, because two ranked nodes can
   507	 * legitimately resolve onto the same row (a merged anchor).
   508	 */
   509	function resolveObjectIds(nodes: EntityNode[], orgId: string | null): string[] {
   510	  const byAnchor = resolveObjectIdsByAnchorNodeUuids(
   511	    nodes.map((n) => n.uuid).filter((u): u is string => typeof u === "string"),
   512	    orgId,
   513	  );
   514	  const ordered: string[] = [];
   515	  const seen = new Set<string>();
   516	  for (const node of nodes) {
   517	    const deterministic = node.uuid ? byAnchor.get(node.uuid) : undefined;
   518	    const candidates = deterministic ? [deterministic] : extractIncidentalObjectIds([node]);
   519	    for (const id of candidates) {
   520	      if (!seen.has(id)) {
   521	        seen.add(id);
   522	        ordered.push(id);
   523	      }
   524	    }
   525	  }
   526	  return ordered;
   527	}
   528	
   529	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   530	// Authorization helpers
   531	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   532	//
   533	// `buildObjectResourceCheck` lifts the canonical owner_level / owner_id /
   534	// visibility off an ObjectRecord into the generic envelope consumed by
   535	// `enforceResourceAccess`. Objects do NOT have a co-owner table, so
   536	// coOwnerUserIds is omitted.
   537	//
   538	// `deriveSaveDefaults` resolves the ownership defaults applied by
   539	// `objects_save` when the caller does not supply explicit ownerLevel / ownerId
   540	// / visibility:
  1280	          scopeActor,
  1281	        );
  1282	        const visible = await filterByAuthz(rows);
  1283	        const items = applyCategoryFilter(visible.map(mapRowToObject));
  1284	        return { items, nextCursor: null };
  1285	      }
  1286	
  1287	      // -----------------------------------------------------------------
  1288	      // With query: Graphiti for ranked IDs, Postgres for canonical rows.
  1289	      // searchNodes failure -> meta.semanticSearch="unavailable" +
  1290	      // meta.fallback="postgres_filter" + body from a Postgres-only list.
  1291	      // -----------------------------------------------------------------
  1292	      const groupId = resolveGroupId(orgId);
  1293	
  1294	      // Scope-recall lane entitlement (cinatra#1379 memory AC4, generalized to
  1295	      // artifact rows in cinatra#1436 AC3). LANE-ELIGIBLE rows — memory concepts
  1296	      // and artifact-scoped rows (the generic artifact type OR a claimed type
  1297	      // resolving to an artifact-safe / faceted disposition) — project into
  1298	      // NESTED per-scope lanes (user / team / ambient, optionally
  1299	      // project-suffixed), so a recall over the ambient org lane alone would
  1300	      // miss the caller's own user-lane and team-lane rows. For a lane-eligible
  1301	      // query we pass the actor's SERVER-DERIVED entitled lane set — own user
  1302	      // lane + a lane for every team the actor is a member of + the org lane,
  1303	      // each also in its `-proj-<id>` form when a projectId is in the call
  1304	      // context. A lane the actor is not entitled to is never in the set, so an
  1305	      // unentitled team's / project's rows can never surface. Every other type
  1306	      // keeps the single ambient org lane (unchanged). The lane set is relevance
  1307	      // scoping only: each candidate is still re-fetched + object.read-gated.
  1308	      let searchGroupIds: string[] = [groupId];
  1309	      const wantsEntitledLanes =
  1310	        input.type != null &&
  1311	        orgId != null &&
  1312	        (input.type === MEMORY_CONCEPT_TYPE_ID || isArtifactScopedRecallType(input.type));
  1313	      if (wantsEntitledLanes) {
  1314	        const actorUserId = actorExt.userId;
  1315	        let teamIds: string[] = [];
  1316	        if (actorUserId && orgId) {
  1317	          const teams = await readTeamsForUser(actorUserId, orgId);
  1318	          teamIds = teams.map((t) => t.id);
  1319	        }
  1320	        searchGroupIds = deriveEntitledLanes({
  1321	          orgId,
  1322	          userId: actorUserId,
  1323	          teamIds,
  1324	          projectId,
  1325	        });
  1326	      }
  1327	
  1328	      let objectIds: string[] | null = null;
  1329	      let degraded = false;
  1330	      try {
  1331	        const res = await searchNodes({
  1332	          query: input.query!,
  1333	          group_ids: searchGroupIds,
  1334	          max_nodes: input.limit ?? 50,
  1335	        });
  1336	        objectIds = resolveObjectIds(res.nodes, orgId);
  1337	      } catch (err) {
  1338	        console.warn(
  1339	          "[objects_list] searchNodes failed; falling back to Postgres-only filter:",
  1340	          err,
  1341	        );
  1342	        degraded = true;
  1343	      }
  1344	
  1345	      if (degraded || objectIds === null || objectIds.length === 0) {
  1346	        const rows = listObjectsByFilter(
  1347	          {
  1348	            orgId,
  1349	            type: input.type,
  1350	            runId: input.runId,
  1351	            dataEquals: input.dataEquals, // cinatra#1456 (Graphiti-fallback path)
  1352	            limit: input.limit,
  1353	            // Sealed-room filter applies on the Graphiti-fallback path too. The
  1354	            // user supplied a projectId; the result must stay inside the project
  1355	            // regardless of search path.
  1356	            projectId,
  1357	          },
  1358	          scopeActor,
  1359	        );
  1360	        const visible = await filterByAuthz(rows);
  1361	        const items = applyCategoryFilter(visible.map(mapRowToObject));
  1362	        // Distinguish "Graphiti unavailable" from "Graphiti responded, and
  1363	        // none of its ranked nodes named a row we hold".
  1364	        //
  1365	        // Since cinatra#2591 that second state means something much sharper
  1366	        // than it used to. Recovery no longer depends on the extraction model
  1367	        // emitting an id: every projected row is seeded as a deterministic
  1368	        // anchor node and resolved through `objects.graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`.
  1369	        // So `no_ids_extracted` now says the hits were genuinely other nodes
  1370	        // (extracted entities from some row's episode, another tenant's lane
  1371	        // filtered out, or rows projected before the anchor existed) — not that
  1372	        // the id field path is broken. It stays classified as DEGRADATION
  1373	        // rather than an empty search result, which is the contract
  1374	        // cinatra#1380's `memory_recall` depends on.
  1375	        const meta = degraded
  1376	          ? { semanticSearch: "unavailable" as const, fallback: "postgres_filter" as const }
  1377	          : objectIds !== null && objectIds.length === 0
  1378	            ? { semanticSearch: "no_ids_extracted" as const, fallback: "postgres_filter" as const }
  1379	            : undefined;
  1380	        return { items, nextCursor: null, ...(meta ? { meta } : {}) };
  1381	      }
  1382	
  1383	      // Fetch canonical rows by ids — listObjectsByFilter suppresses ORDER BY
  1384	      // when `ids` is set so we can preserve the Graphiti rank ourselves.
  1385	      // Ranking is preserved via Map<string, ObjectRecord> — never rows.find()
  1386	      // (Pitfall §5: O(n²) and silently breaks on duplicate ids).
  1387	      //
  1388	      // Graphiti returned ranked candidate IDs that may include rows outside
  1389	      // the requested project. Passing `projectId` here means
  1390	      // `listObjectsByFilter` runs BOTH
packages/objects/src/graphiti-projector.ts:4:import { addEpisode, addTriplet, deleteEpisode, identityHashToUuid } from "./graphiti-client";
