   525	
   526	/**
   527	 * Retract a lane-eligible row's lane bookkeeping after its prior-lane episode
   528	 * has been deleted because the row transitioned to a NON-projected scope
   529	 * (visibility='public' or an unclassifiable scope — #1379 memory, #1436
   530	 * artifact). Clears projected_group_id + graphiti_episode_uuid so a re-enqueue
   531	 * does not re-delete an already-gone episode (prior lane reads back as "none"),
   532	 * and records that the row is no longer projected. graphiti_projected_version is
   533	 * intentionally left untouched (a later scope change back to a real lane bumps
   534	 * the version and re-projects fresh). */
   535	function markLaneRetracted(objectId: string): void {
   536	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   537	  runPostgresQueriesSync({
   538	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   539	    queries: [
   540	      {
   541	        text: `UPDATE "${schema}"."objects"
   542	             SET graphiti_sync_status = 'skipped',
   543	                 graphiti_episode_uuid = NULL,
   544	                 projected_group_id = NULL,
   545	                 graphiti_projected_at = now(),
   546	                 graphiti_projection_error = NULL
   547	             WHERE id = $1`,
   548	        values: [objectId],
   549	      },
   550	    ],
   551	  });
   552	}
   553	
   554	/**
   555	 * Retract a row that transitions to a NON-projected state — a lane-derivation
   556	 * terminal skip (public / unclassifiable scope) OR a winning claim disposition
   557	 * of 'none'. Best-effort purge the PRIOR-lane episode (located via the PERSISTED
   558	 * projected_group_id, never re-derived) and clear the lane bookkeeping so a
   559	 * re-enqueue does not re-attempt the purge. A row that was never projected
   560	 * (priorLane null) just logs the skip. Best-effort + swallowed: the purge is
   561	 * relevance HYGIENE, not the authz gate (recall re-checks Postgres ownership +
   562	 * object.read on the CURRENT scope), so a delete failure never blocks the skip.
   563	 * Shared by the claim-'none' skip and the lane-skip so an artifact-safe -> none
   564	 * disposition flip never leaves a nested-lane episode orphaned (#1436).
   565	 */
   566	async function retractPriorLaneEpisode(row: CanonicalRow, reason: string): Promise<void> {
   567	  const priorLane = row.projected_group_id;
   568	  if (!priorLane) {
   569	    console.log(`[graphiti-projector] skipping row ${row.id} (${row.type}): ${reason}`);
   570	    return;
   571	  }
   572	  try {
   573	    await deleteEpisode({ uuid: identityHashToUuid(row.id, priorLane) });
   574	  } catch (err) {
   575	    console.warn(
   576	      `[graphiti-projector] lane retract: deleteEpisode failed for prior lane ${priorLane} of ${row.id}:`,
   577	      err,
   578	    );
   579	  }
   580	  markLaneRetracted(row.id);
   581	  console.log(
   582	    `[graphiti-projector] row ${row.id} (${row.type}): retracted prior-lane episode (${priorLane}) — ${reason}`,
   583	  );
   584	}
   585	
   586	function markDeleted(objectId: string): void {
   587	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   588	  runPostgresQueriesSync({
   589	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   590	    queries: [
   591	      {
   592	        text: `UPDATE "${schema}"."objects"
   593	             SET graphiti_sync_status = 'deleted',
   594	                 graphiti_projected_at = now(),
   595	                 graphiti_projection_error = NULL
   596	             WHERE id = $1`,
   597	        values: [objectId],
   598	      },
   599	    ],
   600	  });
   601	}
   602	
   603	/**
   604	 * Project an object to Graphiti as a NEW append-only episode.
   605	 * Calls addEpisode only; never deleteEpisode + addEpisode.
   606	 *
   607	 * Returns `{ episodeUuid: string }` on success, or `{ episodeUuid: null,
   608	 * skipped: true }` when the canonical row's version has already advanced
   609	 * past this outbox entry (stale-outbox guard).
   610	 */
   760	  // Group-id (Graphiti lane) derivation. LANE-ELIGIBLE rows NEST under the org
   761	  // lane via their server-derived scope: memory concepts (#1379 AC1) UNION
   762	  // generic artifact rows UNION claimed rows whose winning disposition is
   763	  // artifact-safe / faceted (#1436 AC1). A claimed 'raw' explicit opt-in,
   764	  // adapter-owned CRM rows (routed to the adapter above and never reaching
   765	  // here), and every other typed row keep the SINGLE ambient org lane (epic
   766	  // #1424 guardrail — deriveProjectionGroupId stays single-lane-per-org for
   767	  // those). All lane-eligible classes reuse the SAME hardened scope->lane
   768	  // function (deriveScopeLane) — no independent artifact branch that could
   769	  // reintroduce the #1379 phantom-team-lane bug (#1436 AC6).
   770	  const isMemory = row.type === MEMORY_CONCEPT_TYPE_ID;
   771	  const isLaneEligible =
   772	    isMemory ||
   773	    row.type === GENERIC_ARTIFACT_OBJECT_TYPE ||
   774	    claimedProjection?.kind === "faceted";
   775	  // The prior lane is the PERSISTED `projected_group_id` (NOT re-derived from
   776	  // the current scope — that already reflects the NEW scope). The lane-scoped
   777	  // deterministic UUID (identityHashToUuid(row.id, priorLane)) locates the
   778	  // prior-lane episode for the best-effort purge below.
   779	  //
   780	  // SECURITY BOUNDARY NOTE. The Graphiti lane is a RECALL-RELEVANCE scoping
   781	  // mechanism, NOT the authorization boundary. The authz boundary is the
   782	  // recall handler's canonical re-fetch: objects_list resolves searchNodes
   783	  // candidate ids, then `listObjectsByFilter(..., scopeActor)` re-reads the
   784	  // Postgres rows through `buildOwnershipFilter` (owner_level/owner_id/
   785	  // visibility) and `enforceResourceAccess(object.read)` re-checks each — on
   786	  // the CURRENT scope. So a stale/orphaned episode in an old lane can at worst
   787	  // surface a candidate id that the Postgres ownership filter then DROPS; it
   788	  // can never disclose content under a scope the actor no longer has. The
   789	  // episode purge here is therefore relevance HYGIENE, and it is best-effort:
   790	  // Graphiti gives no episode-uuid control (see graphiti-rebuild.ts — the
   791	  // authoritative purge is a group rebuild, not per-episode delete), exactly
   792	  // like `deleteCurrentEpisodeFromGraphiti`. A delete failure must NOT block
   793	  // re-projection (that would strand the row out of its new lane), so it is
   794	  // swallowed + warned, mirroring the object-delete path.
   795	  const priorLane = row.projected_group_id;
   796	  let groupId: string;
   797	  if (isLaneEligible) {
   798	    const derivation = deriveScopeLane(input.orgId ?? row.org_id, {
   799	      ownerLevel: row.owner_level,
   800	      ownerId: row.owner_id,
   801	      visibility: row.visibility,
   802	      projectId: row.project_id,
   803	    });
   804	    if (derivation.kind === "skip") {
   805	      // Terminal skip (public / unclassifiable scope). Best-effort purge any
   806	      // prior-lane episode + clear the bookkeeping (shared with the claim-'none'
   807	      // skip). Recall authz is enforced Postgres-side on the current scope
   808	      // regardless.
   809	      await retractPriorLaneEpisode(row, derivation.reason);
   810	      return { episodeUuid: null, skipped: true };
   811	    }
   812	    groupId = derivation.groupId;
   813	  } else {
   814	    groupId = deriveProjectionGroupId(input.orgId ?? row.org_id);
   815	  }
   816	  // Lane-move (#1379 AC2, generalized to artifact rows #1436 AC2). When the prior
   817	  // lane differs from the newly-derived lane — a scope change between nested
   818	  // lanes, OR a claimed row leaving lane treatment entirely because its winning
   819	  // disposition flipped artifact-safe -> raw (nested lane -> the ambient org
   820	  // lane) — best-effort purge the prior-lane episode (its lane-scoped UUID
   821	  // differs from the new one) before projecting into the new lane. Applies to
   822	  // BOTH branches so a lane-eligibility EXIT never strands a nested episode.
   823	  // Best-effort + swallowed for the same reason as the retract above — the purge
   824	  // is relevance hygiene, not the authz gate. A first-ever projection (priorLane
   825	  // null) and a same-lane re-projection (priorLane === groupId) purge nothing.
   826	  if (priorLane && priorLane !== groupId) {
   827	    try {
   828	      await deleteEpisode({ uuid: identityHashToUuid(row.id, priorLane) });
   829	      console.log(
   830	        `[graphiti-projector] lane-move for ${row.id} (${row.type}): purged prior-lane episode ` +
   831	          `(${priorLane} -> ${groupId})`,
   832	      );
   833	    } catch (err) {
   834	      console.warn(
   835	        `[graphiti-projector] lane-move: deleteEpisode failed for prior lane ${priorLane} of ${row.id}:`,
   836	        err,
   837	      );
   838	    }
   839	  }
   840	  // EPISODE-UUID-EMPTY: knowledge-graph-mcp 1.0.x add_memory returns only a
   841	  // message string — no uuid in any known response path. We compute a stable
   842	  // deterministic UUID locally for Postgres bookkeeping and delete attempts.
   843	  // NOTE: do NOT pass uuid to addEpisode. Graphiti 0.28.2 queue_service
   844	  // interprets the uuid param as "re-process an existing node" — it issues
   845	  // MATCH (uuid) which fails with "node not found" when the episode is new,
   846	  // permanently blocking its processing. Episodes must be created without uuid.
   847	  //
   848	  // Embed [oid:<objectId>] in the episode name so it travels with
   849	  // the episode record. NOTE: live verification (2026-04-30) showed this tag does
   850	  // NOT propagate to entity node names via LLM extraction, and re-checking on
   900	    },
   901	  });
   902	
   903	  const entityName = deriveEntityName(row.data, row.type);
   904	
   905	  await addEpisode({
   906	    name: `${entityName} [oid:${row.id}]`,
   907	    episode_body: episodeBody,
   908	    source: "json",
   909	    source_description: `objects projection (run ${row.run_id ?? "n/a"})`,
   910	    group_id: groupId,
   911	    // uuid intentionally omitted — see EPISODE-UUID-EMPTY note above
   912	  });
   913	
   914	  const anchorNodeUuid = await seedAnchorNode({
   915	    objectId: row.id,
   916	    groupId,
   917	    entityName,
   918	    type: row.type,
   919	  });
   920	
   921	  markProjected({
   922	    objectId: row.id,
   923	    episodeUuid,
   924	    projectedVersion: input.objectVersion,
   925	    // Persist the lane just projected into so a later scope change can locate
   926	    // and delete THIS episode (#1379 lane-move bookkeeping).
   927	    projectedGroupId: groupId,
   928	    anchorNodeUuid,
   929	  });
   930	  return { episodeUuid, anchorNodeUuid };
   931	}
   932	
   933	// Caller note:
   934	// `processProjectionOutbox` must treat `{ skipped: true }` as a successful
   935	// outcome and mark the corresponding outbox row `done` (not `failed`). The
   936	// loop body in this file already wraps the call in try/catch — the success
   937	// path falls through to the "Mark outbox row done" UPDATE, which is the
   938	// correct behavior for skipped entries (do not retry; a newer outbox row
   939	// will deliver the latest state). No special-casing needed in the worker
   940	// loop, but executors MUST verify the resolved value is awaited (not the
   941	// promise) so the skipped flag is observable.
   942	
   943	/**
   944	 * Delete the current episode pointer (does NOT hard-delete extracted entities).
   945	 */
   946	export async function deleteCurrentEpisodeFromGraphiti(input: {
   947	  objectId: string;
   948	  orgId: string | null;
   949	}): Promise<void> {
   950	  const row = readCanonicalRow(input.objectId, input.orgId);
   951	  if (!row) return; // already gone
   952	  if (row.graphiti_episode_uuid) {
   953	    try {
   954	      await deleteEpisode({ uuid: row.graphiti_episode_uuid });
   955	    } catch (err) {
   956	      console.warn(
   957	        `[graphiti-projector] deleteEpisode failed for ${input.objectId}:`,
   958	        err,
   959	      );
   960	      // Continue — we still mark deleted in PG; the cleanup CLI handles orphans.
   961	    }
   962	  }
   963	  markDeleted(row.id);
   964	}
   965	
   966	/**
   967	 * Repair worker: claim batch via FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, project each row,
   968	 * mark outbox rows done/failed.
   969	 */
   970	export async function processProjectionOutbox(options?: {
   586	  return { clauses, nextIndex: pIdx };
   587	}
   588	
   589	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   590	// Deterministic graph-node -> canonical-row resolution (cinatra#2591).
   591	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   592	//
   593	// `search_nodes` ranks and returns entity-node UUIDs. This turns them back into
   594	// canonical object ids by reading the DETERMINISTIC anchor node each projected
   595	// row is seeded as (`graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`, written by the projector).
   596	//
   597	// It replaces a chain of four probes that read an id off whatever the
   598	// extraction model happened to emit — three of which were already measured
   599	// inert against the graph, and the fourth of which fired only when the model
   600	// incidentally produced the row UUID as an entity name. That chain is why
   601	// recall could answer `no_ids_extracted` for a row that was definitely indexed.
   602	//
   603	// AUTHORIZATION IS DELIBERATELY NOT HERE. This is id resolution only: it takes
   604	// UUIDs the graph already returned and says which rows they name. Every
   605	// resolved id is then re-fetched through `listObjectsByFilter` with the caller's
   606	// actor, which applies the ownership filter, the sealed-room project clause and
   607	// the soft-delete filter in SQL. That is the same shape the Graphiti-extracted
   608	// ids already flowed through, so the read boundary is unchanged — this only
   609	// makes the candidate set correct.
   610	//
   611	// Returns a Map so the caller can preserve the graph's RANK order (a plain
   612	// array of rows would lose it; `rows.find()` per uuid would be O(n^2)).
   613	export function resolveObjectIdsByAnchorNodeUuids(
   614	  nodeUuids: readonly string[],
   615	  orgId: string | null,
   616	): Map<string, string> {
   617	  const resolved = new Map<string, string>();
   618	  const unique = [...new Set(nodeUuids.filter((u) => typeof u === "string" && u.length > 0))];
   619	  if (unique.length === 0) return resolved;
   620	
   621	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   622	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   623	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   624	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   625	    queries: [
   626	      {
   627	        // `(org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL)` mirrors listObjectsByFilter's own org
   628	        // clause, so an org-scoped recall can never resolve a UUID onto another
   629	        // tenant's row even before the actor filter runs.
   630	        text: `SELECT id, graphiti_anchor_node_uuid
   631	FROM "${schema}"."objects"
   632	WHERE graphiti_anchor_node_uuid = ANY($1::text[])
   633	  AND (org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL)
   634	  AND deleted_at IS NULL`,
   635	        values: [unique, orgId],
   636	      },
   637	    ],
   638	  });
   639	
   640	  for (const row of result?.rows ?? []) {
   641	    const nodeUuid = (row as { graphiti_anchor_node_uuid?: unknown }).graphiti_anchor_node_uuid;
   642	    const id = (row as { id?: unknown }).id;
   643	    if (typeof nodeUuid === "string" && typeof id === "string") resolved.set(nodeUuid, id);
   644	  }
   645	  return resolved;
   646	}
   647	
   648	export function listObjectsByFilter(
   649	  filter: ListObjectsFilter,
   650	  actor?: ActorContext,
   651	): ObjectRecord[] {
   652	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   653	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   654	
   655	  const where: string[] = [`(org_id = $1 OR $1 IS NULL)`, `deleted_at IS NULL`];
   452	// Graphiti search_nodes -> objectId extraction
   453	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   454	//
   455	// DETERMINISTIC FIRST, incidental second (cinatra#2591 deliverable 4).
   456	//
   457	// WHAT THIS USED TO BE. A chain of four probes that read a row id off whatever
   458	// Graphiti's extraction model happened to put on an entity node. Three of them
   459	// were measured INERT against Graphiti 0.28.2 (live verification 2026-04-30 —
   460	// custom episode-body fields do not reach node attributes, and the `[oid:…]`
   461	// name tag does not survive extraction). The fourth fired only when the model
   462	// incidentally emitted the row UUID as an entity-node name. So a recall could
   463	// report `no_ids_extracted` for a row that was definitely indexed, and the
   464	// planned `memory_recall` semantic path (cinatra#1380) would have inherited
   465	// that.
   466	//
   467	// WHAT IT IS NOW. Every projected row is seeded as a DETERMINISTIC anchor node
   468	// whose UUID is a pure function of the row id and the lane
   469	// (`graphiti-projector.ts` -> `anchorNodeUuidFor`), and the inverse map lives in
   470	// Postgres (`objects.graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`). Ranked node UUIDs resolve
   471	// through that column. Nothing depends on model whim.
   472	//
   473	// THE LEGACY PROBES ARE KEPT, DEMOTED. They are the ONLY recovery path for rows
   474	// projected before this change (their anchor column is null until the next
   475	// projection) and for adapter-owned rows that project through their own
   476	// episode. They run second and only add ids the deterministic pass did not
   477	// already produce. They are not the mechanism; they are the tail.
   478	const OID_RE = /\[oid:([0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12})\]/;
   479	const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
   480	
   481	/** The pre-#2591 chain: ids read off model-produced node fields. Inert for most
   482	 *  nodes by measurement; retained for rows with no anchor yet. */
   483	function extractIncidentalObjectIds(nodes: EntityNode[]): string[] {
   484	  const ids: string[] = [];
   485	  for (const n of nodes) {
   486	    const attrs = (n as unknown as { attributes?: Record<string, unknown> })
   487	      .attributes;
   488	    const labels = (n as unknown as { labels?: string[] }).labels;
   489	    const candidate =
   490	      (attrs?.cinatra_object_id as unknown) ??
   491	      (n as unknown as { cinatra_object_id?: unknown }).cinatra_object_id ??
   492	      OID_RE.exec(n.name)?.[1] ??
   493	      (labels?.includes("Object") && UUID_RE.test(n.name ?? "") ? n.name : undefined);
   494	    if (typeof candidate === "string" && candidate.length > 0) {
   495	      ids.push(candidate);
   496	    }
   497	  }
   498	  return ids;
   499	}
   500	
   501	/**
   502	 * Ranked entity nodes -> canonical object ids, RANK PRESERVED.
   503	 *
   504	 * Walks the nodes in the order Graphiti ranked them and emits, per node, the
   505	 * deterministically-anchored row id when there is one, otherwise whatever the
   506	 * incidental probes can recover. De-duplicated, because two ranked nodes can
   507	 * legitimately resolve onto the same row (a merged anchor).
   508	 */
   509	function resolveObjectIds(nodes: EntityNode[], orgId: string | null): string[] {
   510	  const byAnchor = resolveObjectIdsByAnchorNodeUuids(
   511	    nodes.map((n) => n.uuid).filter((u): u is string => typeof u === "string"),
   512	    orgId,
   513	  );
   514	  const ordered: string[] = [];
   515	  const seen = new Set<string>();
   516	  for (const node of nodes) {
   517	    const deterministic = node.uuid ? byAnchor.get(node.uuid) : undefined;
   518	    const candidates = deterministic ? [deterministic] : extractIncidentalObjectIds([node]);
   519	    for (const id of candidates) {
   520	      if (!seen.has(id)) {
   521	        seen.add(id);
   522	        ordered.push(id);
   523	      }
   524	    }
   525	  }
   526	  return ordered;
   527	}
   528	
   529	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   530	// Authorization helpers
  1320	        searchGroupIds = deriveEntitledLanes({
  1321	          orgId,
  1322	          userId: actorUserId,
  1323	          teamIds,
  1324	          projectId,
  1325	        });
  1326	      }
  1327	
  1328	      let objectIds: string[] | null = null;
  1329	      let degraded = false;
  1330	      try {
  1331	        const res = await searchNodes({
  1332	          query: input.query!,
  1333	          group_ids: searchGroupIds,
  1334	          max_nodes: input.limit ?? 50,
  1335	        });
  1336	        objectIds = resolveObjectIds(res.nodes, orgId);
  1337	      } catch (err) {
  1338	        console.warn(
  1339	          "[objects_list] searchNodes failed; falling back to Postgres-only filter:",
  1340	          err,
  1341	        );
  1342	        degraded = true;
  1343	      }
  1344	
  1345	      if (degraded || objectIds === null || objectIds.length === 0) {
  1346	        const rows = listObjectsByFilter(
  1347	          {
  1348	            orgId,
  1349	            type: input.type,
  1350	            runId: input.runId,
  1351	            dataEquals: input.dataEquals, // cinatra#1456 (Graphiti-fallback path)
  1352	            limit: input.limit,
  1353	            // Sealed-room filter applies on the Graphiti-fallback path too. The
  1354	            // user supplied a projectId; the result must stay inside the project
  1355	            // regardless of search path.
  1356	            projectId,
  1357	          },
  1358	          scopeActor,
  1359	        );
  1360	        const visible = await filterByAuthz(rows);
  1361	        const items = applyCategoryFilter(visible.map(mapRowToObject));
  1362	        // Distinguish "Graphiti unavailable" from "Graphiti responded, and
  1363	        // none of its ranked nodes named a row we hold".
  1364	        //
  1365	        // Since cinatra#2591 that second state means something much sharper
  1366	        // than it used to. Recovery no longer depends on the extraction model
  1367	        // emitting an id: every projected row is seeded as a deterministic
  1368	        // anchor node and resolved through `objects.graphiti_anchor_node_uuid`.
  1369	        // So `no_ids_extracted` now says the hits were genuinely other nodes
  1370	        // (extracted entities from some row's episode, another tenant's lane
  1371	        // filtered out, or rows projected before the anchor existed) — not that
  1372	        // the id field path is broken. It stays classified as DEGRADATION
  1373	        // rather than an empty search result, which is the contract
  1374	        // cinatra#1380's `memory_recall` depends on.
  1375	        const meta = degraded
  1376	          ? { semanticSearch: "unavailable" as const, fallback: "postgres_filter" as const }
  1377	          : objectIds !== null && objectIds.length === 0
  1378	            ? { semanticSearch: "no_ids_extracted" as const, fallback: "postgres_filter" as const }
  1379	            : undefined;
  1380	        return { items, nextCursor: null, ...(meta ? { meta } : {}) };
  1381	      }
  1382	
  1383	      // Fetch canonical rows by ids — listObjectsByFilter suppresses ORDER BY
  1384	      // when `ids` is set so we can preserve the Graphiti rank ourselves.
  1385	      // Ranking is preserved via Map<string, ObjectRecord> — never rows.find()
    65	const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
    66	const repoRoot = path.resolve(__dirname, "..");
    67	
    68	// The keys that carry the resolved provider KEY. It uses pydantic-settings
    69	// (`__` nested delimiter) for its own config AND graphiti_core reads the bare
    70	// OPENAI_API_KEY during internal initialization, so all three carry the same
    71	// value. Every other setting (database wiring, SEMAPHORE_LIMIT, CONFIG_PATH) is
    72	// static and stays in the compose `environment:` block: those are not
    73	// interpolated, share no key with this file, and so never hit the
    74	// empty-override trap.
    75	export const GRAPHITI_KEY_NAMES = [
    76	  "LLM__PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY",
    77	  "EMBEDDER__PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY",
    78	  "OPENAI_API_KEY",
    79	];

    81	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    82	// THE EMBEDDER FLOOR, AND A BOOT CRASH IT ALSO FIXES (cinatra#2591).
    83	//
    84	// EMBEDDER. Graphiti ranks on embeddings, and every embedder provider it
    85	// supports is a paid hosted API (Anthropic has none at all). So when the app has
    86	// a usable OpenAI key, the embedder rides that key; otherwise it falls back to
    87	// the LOCAL `kg-embedder` service, which speaks the OpenAI `/v1/embeddings`
    88	// contract over a baked-in sentence-transformers model. Upstream's OpenAI
    89	// embedder branch forwards `base_url` to the SDK precisely so a local endpoint
    90	// can stand in.
    91	//
    92	// The floor is not a consolation prize: since cinatra#2591 seeds a deterministic
    93	// anchor node per projected row, RANKING depends on the embedder and not on
    94	// extraction — so an install on the local floor ranks its own rows even with no
    95	// extraction provider at all.
    96	//
    97	// THE CRASH. Measured on the wire 2026-08-10 against the replacement server:
    98	// with `llm.provider = openai` and NO key, the server does not degrade — it
    99	// dies at startup. `LLMClientFactory` warns and returns no client (fine), but
   100	// `CrossEncoderFactory` then builds an `OpenAIRerankerClient` from the SAME
   101	// provider block regardless, and `AsyncOpenAI(api_key=None)` raises
   102	// `OpenAIError: Missing credentials`, taking the whole process down:
   103	//
   104	//   WARNING  Failed to create LLM client: OpenAI API key is not configured.
   105	//   INFO     Using OpenAIRerankerClient from LLM provider
   106	//   ERROR    Failed to initialize Graphiti client: Missing credentials. …
   107	//
   108	// A keyless install must not have a crash-looping indexer, so the keyless file
   109	// carries an explicit SENTINEL for the LLM key. The sentinel is a string, never
   110	// a credential; it is named for what it means so it reads as an explanation
   111	// wherever it surfaces. It cannot buy an extraction call — any request made with
   112	// it is rejected by OpenAI — and nothing in cinatra treats its presence as
   113	// "configured": the app's own state still reads `absent`, because that answer
   114	// comes from `readKnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState`, not from this file.
   115	//
   116	// The reranker it keeps alive is never CALLED on cinatra's path: the recall
   117	// query uses NODE_HYBRID_SEARCH_RRF (reciprocal-rank fusion, no cross-encoder).
   118	// This is purely about letting the server boot.
   119	export const GRAPHITI_NO_LLM_SENTINEL = "cinatra-no-extraction-provider-configured";
   120	
   121	/** Where the local embedder answers on the compose network. */
   122	export const LOCAL_EMBEDDER_API_URL = "http://kg-embedder:8080/v1";
   123	/** Model + width baked into docker/kg-embedder. `dimensions` MUST match the
   124	 *  model: graphiti declares the width up front and a mismatch produces vectors
   125	 *  the store compares silently wrongly rather than loudly. */
   126	export const LOCAL_EMBEDDER_MODEL = "bge-small-en-v1.5";
   127	export const LOCAL_EMBEDDER_DIMENSIONS = "384";
   128	/** The OpenAI SDK refuses to construct without a non-empty key, and the local
   129	 *  service ignores `Authorization` entirely (it has no auth — it is never
   130	 *  published to a host interface). This is a placeholder, not a credential. */
   131	export const LOCAL_EMBEDDER_PLACEHOLDER_KEY = "cinatra-local-embedder";
   132	
   133	const HOSTED_EMBEDDER_MODEL = "text-embedding-3-small";
   134	const HOSTED_EMBEDDER_DIMENSIONS = "1536";
   135	
   136	// Parse a dotenv file into a flat object. Mirrors gen-nango-env.mjs /
   137	// gen-wayflow-env.mjs (same regex) so the parsers never drift.
   138	export function parseDotenv(contents) {
   139	  const env = {};
   140	  for (const line of contents.split("\n")) {
   141	    const match = line.match(/^\s*([A-Z0-9_]+)\s*=\s*(.*?)\s*$/);
   142	    if (match) env[match[1]] = match[2].replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
   143	  }
   144	  return env;
   145	}
   146	
   147	// Build the narrow env map from ONE resolved key. Pure (no IO), so the shape is
   148	// unit-testable without a database. A null/blank key yields an EMPTY map — not
   149	// three empty-string entries, which is exactly the silent behaviour this file
   150	// exists to end.
   151	//
   152	// Surrounding whitespace is trimmed first (a trailing newline from a paste is
   153	// not a corrupt key). A control character that survives the trim — anywhere
   154	// INSIDE the value — is then REFUSED, not written: dotenv is line-oriented, so
   155	// an embedded newline would either truncate the credential or inject an extra
   156	// variable into the container's environment. Refusing degrades to the honest
   157	// keyless state, which is strictly safer than a half-written credential.
   158	export function buildGraphitiEnv(apiKey) {
   159	  const key = typeof apiKey === "string" ? apiKey.trim() : "";
   160	  const rejected = key && /[\u0000-\u001F\u007F]/.test(key) ? "control-character" : null;
   161	  const usable = rejected ? "" : key;
   162	
   163	  if (!usable) {
   164	    // KEYLESS. No longer an EMPTY map: the replacement server needs the LLM
   165	    // sentinel to boot at all (see GRAPHITI_NO_LLM_SENTINEL above), and the
   166	    // embedder is pointed at the local floor so the install still ranks its own
   167	    // rows through their deterministic anchors. What is still absent is the one
   168	    // thing that was ever absent: a real extraction key. No secret is written.
   169	    return {
   170	      env: {
   171	        LLM__PROVIDER: "openai",
   172	        LLM__PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY: GRAPHITI_NO_LLM_SENTINEL,
   173	        EMBEDDER__PROVIDER: "openai",
   174	        EMBEDDER__MODEL: LOCAL_EMBEDDER_MODEL,
   175	        EMBEDDER__DIMENSIONS: LOCAL_EMBEDDER_DIMENSIONS,
   176	        EMBEDDER__PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_URL: LOCAL_EMBEDDER_API_URL,
   177	        EMBEDDER__PROVIDERS__OPENAI__API_KEY: LOCAL_EMBEDDER_PLACEHOLDER_KEY,
   178	      },
   179	      hasKey: false,
   180	      embedder: "local",
   181	      rejected,
   182	    };
   183	  }
   184	
   185	  // KEYED. Extraction and embeddings both ride the configured OpenAI key.
   186	  const env = {};
   187	  for (const name of GRAPHITI_KEY_NAMES) env[name] = usable;
   188	  env.LLM__PROVIDER = "openai";
   189	  env.EMBEDDER__PROVIDER = "openai";
   190	  env.EMBEDDER__MODEL = HOSTED_EMBEDDER_MODEL;
   191	  env.EMBEDDER__DIMENSIONS = HOSTED_EMBEDDER_DIMENSIONS;
   192	  return { env, hasKey: true, embedder: "openai", rejected: null };
   193	}
   194	
   195	// Does the file already carry a usable key? Pure (no IO). The caller combines
   196	// this with "could the stored configuration be read" to decide preserve vs
   197	// rewrite — see generateGraphitiEnv.
   198	export function shouldPreserveExisting(existingContents) {
   199	  const parsed = parseDotenv(existingContents ?? "");
   200	  return GRAPHITI_KEY_NAMES.some((name) => {
   201	    const value = typeof parsed[name] === "string" ? parsed[name].trim() : "";
   202	    // The keyless file is no longer empty (it carries the boot sentinel and the
   203	    // local-embedder placeholder), so "is a key materialized" can no longer be
   204	    // "is anything present". Both known non-credentials are excluded by value:
   205	    // treating either as a real key would make the cold-start guard preserve a
   206	    // keyless file forever and hide a key the operator then configured.
   207	    if (!value) return false;
   208	    if (value === GRAPHITI_NO_LLM_SENTINEL) return false;
   209	    if (value === LOCAL_EMBEDDER_PLACEHOLDER_KEY) return false;
   210	    return true;
   211	  });
   212	}
   213	
   214	// Serialize to dotenv text (bare values — compose's env_file parser treats the
   215	// post-`=` remainder as the literal value; quoting would embed the quotes).
   216	export function serializeDotenv(env) {
   217	  const header =
   218	    "# GENERATED by scripts/gen-graphiti-env.mjs — DO NOT EDIT.\n" +
   219	    "# The OpenAI key the knowledge-graph indexer runs with, resolved from the\n" +
   220	    "# app's stored provider configuration. Gitignored, 0600.\n";
   221	  const body = Object.entries(env)
   222	    .map(([k, v]) => `${k}=${v}`)
   223	    .join("\n");
   224	  return `${header}${body}${body ? "\n" : ""}`;
   225	}
   226	
   227	/**
   228	 * Resolve the key through the app's own resolver.
   229	 *
   230	 * Imported LAZILY and tolerantly: the resolver is TypeScript that reaches the
   231	 * database, so (a) the unit tests can import the pure helpers above without it,
   232	 * and (b) a bring-up whose database is not up yet degrades to "no key" instead
   233	 * of crashing the bring-up.
   234	 */
   235	async function resolveKey() {
   255	 * "the key lands in the container's env", and a mocked writer would not make it.
   256	 *
   257	 * @param {{outPath: string, resolveKey?: () => Promise<{key: string|null, reason: string}>,
   258	 *          log?: (msg: string) => void, warn?: (msg: string) => void}} options
   259	 * @returns {Promise<{state: "configured"|"absent", wrote: boolean, keyCount: number,
   260	 *                     embedder: "openai"|"local"}>}
   261	 */
   262	export async function generateGraphitiEnv({
   263	  outPath,
   264	  resolveKey: resolve = resolveKey,
   265	  log = console.log,
   266	  warn = console.warn,
   267	} = {}) {
   268	  const resolved = await resolve();
   269	  const { env, hasKey, embedder, rejected } = buildGraphitiEnv(resolved.key);
   270	
   271	  if (rejected === "control-character") {
   272	    warn(
   273	      "[gen-graphiti-env] REFUSED: the resolved provider key contains a control character " +
   274	        "(a newline or similar). A dotenv value is line-oriented, so writing it would " +
   275	        "truncate the credential or inject an extra container variable. Re-save the key " +
   276	        "in the app without stray whitespace.",
   277	    );
   278	  }
   279	
   280	  // PRESERVE whenever the PREFERRED source — the app's stored configuration —
   281	  // could not be read and something usable is already materialized. That covers
   282	  // the cold bring-up (no database yet) and a key that will not decrypt, and it
   283	  // deliberately covers the case where an environment FALLBACK did resolve:
   284	  // replacing a known-good stored key with a possibly-stale env key during a
   285	  // transient database outage is a silent downgrade. The fallback is for when
   286	  // there is nothing materialized to keep.
   287	  //
   288	  // What does NOT preserve: a readable configuration holding no key. That is a
   289	  // disconnect or a rotation, and it must reach the container — otherwise a
   290	  // revoked credential keeps running in it indefinitely.
   291	  const couldNotAsk = resolved.storedReadFailed === true || rejected !== null;
   292	  const materialized =
   293	    existsSync(outPath) && shouldPreserveExisting(readFileSync(outPath, "utf8"));
   294	  if (couldNotAsk && materialized) {
   295	    warn(
   296	      "[gen-graphiti-env] WARNING: the stored provider configuration could not be read " +
   297	        `this run (${resolved.reason}), but the existing ${path.basename(outPath)} still ` +
   298	        "carries a key — keeping it untouched (rewriting it would risk turning " +
   299	        "knowledge-graph indexing off, or downgrading to a stale fallback key).",
   300	    );
   301	    return { state: "configured", wrote: false, keyCount: 0, embedder: "openai" };
   302	  }
   303	
   304	  mkdirSync(path.dirname(outPath), { recursive: true });
   305	  // ATOMIC replace: a fresh 0600 temp file in the same directory, chmod
   306	  // defensively (`mode` only applies on CREATE), then rename over outPath, so
   307	  // readers see the old or the new file and never a truncated one. The temp
   308	  // name shares the ignored `.graphiti.env*` prefix, and a failure between the
   309	  // write and the rename unlinks it rather than leaving a plaintext credential
   310	  // lying in the working tree.
   311	  const tmpPath = `${outPath}.tmp-${process.pid}`;
   312	  try {
   313	    writeFileSync(tmpPath, serializeDotenv(env), { mode: 0o600 });
   314	    chmodSync(tmpPath, 0o600);
   315	    renameSync(tmpPath, outPath);
   316	  } catch (err) {
   317	    try {
   318	      rmSync(tmpPath, { force: true });
   319	    } catch {
   320	      // Best effort — the surviving temp file is gitignored either way.
   321	    }
   322	    throw err;
   323	  }
   324	
   325	  if (hasKey) {
   326	    log(
   327	      `[gen-graphiti-env] knowledge-graph provider key CONFIGURED — ${resolved.reason}; ` +
   328	        `wrote ${outPath} (${GRAPHITI_KEY_NAMES.length} keys, 0600; embedder: ${embedder}). ` +
   329	        "The indexer container picks it up when it is (re)created.",
   330	    );
   331	    return {
   332	      state: "configured",
   333	      wrote: true,
   334	      keyCount: GRAPHITI_KEY_NAMES.length,
   335	      embedder,
   336	    };
   337	  }
   338	  warn(
   339	    `[gen-graphiti-env] knowledge-graph EXTRACTION is OFF — ${resolved.reason}. ` +
   340	      "Episodes are accepted by the indexer and then dropped (extraction runs before the " +
   341	      "graph write), so no entities or facts are extracted. Rows ARE still seeded as " +
   342	      "deterministic anchor nodes and ranked on the LOCAL embedder, so recall over your own " +
   343	      "rows keeps working — what you lose is extracted entities and relationships. " +
   344	      "Configure an OpenAI provider key in the app, then re-run this bring-up " +
   345	      "(`npm run kg:refresh`).",
   346	  );
   347	  return { state: "absent", wrote: true, keyCount: 0, embedder };
   348	}
   349	
   350	// Only run main when invoked directly (not when imported by tests).
   351	if (process.argv[1] && path.resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
   352	  await generateGraphitiEnv({
   353	    outPath: path.join(repoRoot, "docker", "graphiti", ".graphiti.env"),
   354	  });
   355	}
   500	      retries: 10

   502	  # The vendor-free EMBEDDING FLOOR (cinatra#2591). Graphiti ranks on embeddings
   503	  # and every embedder provider it supports is a paid hosted API; Anthropic has
   504	  # no embeddings API at all. Without a local floor, an Anthropic-only install
   505	  # would have to sign up with a SECOND vendor purely to get vectors. This
   506	  # service speaks the OpenAI `/v1/embeddings` contract over a local
   507	  # sentence-transformers model (ONNX, weights baked at build time), so the
   508	  # indexer's embedder can point at it exactly as it would at Ollama.
   509	  #
   510	  # NO `ports:` ON PURPOSE — it is reachable only on the compose network. It has
   511	  # no authentication because it is never published to a host interface; giving
   512	  # it a host binding would be the change that makes that a problem.
   513	  kg-embedder:
   514	    build:
   515	      context: ./docker/kg-embedder
   516	    restart: unless-stopped
   517	    healthcheck:
   518	      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "curl -fsS http://localhost:8080/health || exit 1"]
   519	      interval: 10s

   526	    # docker/graphiti/Dockerfile for the full rationale and both pins.
   527	    #
   528	    # It replaces `zepai/knowledge-graph-mcp:1.0.2-graphiti-0.28.2`. That is the
   529	    # same upstream source, but published on the `mcp-v*` cadence: its newest tag
   530	    # is from 2026-03-11 (graphiti-core 0.28.2) while graphiti-core is at 0.29.3,
   531	    # and re-checking the registry on 2026-08-10 still found no newer tag. Three
   532	    # things this repo now needs do not exist in that build: a custom LLM
   533	    # base-URL (so an OpenAI-compatible stand-in can be used at all), the
   534	    # `get_episode_entities` provenance tool, and a real `GET /health` readiness
   535	    # route.
   536	    #
   537	    # `image:` is a LOCAL tag, not a registry reference — nothing here pushes or
   538	    # pulls it. The tag carries both pins so a stale container is obvious in
   539	    # `docker ps`; bumping either build arg changes the tag in the same edit.
   540	    build:
   541	      context: ./docker/graphiti
   542	    image: cinatra/knowledge-graph-mcp:upstream-425bf248-core-0.29.3
   543	    ports:
   544	      # LOOPBACK ONLY (cinatra#2591). This used to be `8000:8000`, which binds
   545	      # 0.0.0.0 and publishes an UNAUTHENTICATED MCP server — full read/write on
   546	      # the knowledge graph, including `clear_graph` — to every interface of the
   547	      # host. Nothing outside this compose network is a legitimate client: the
   548	      # app reaches it as `http://graphiti:8000` over the internal network, and
   549	      # the only other consumer is a developer on the box. So the binding stays,
   550	      # scoped to 127.0.0.1.
   551	      - "127.0.0.1:8000:8000"
   552	    # PROVIDER CONFIG (cinatra#2582, extended to multi-provider by cinatra#2591)
   553	    # — sourced from a NARROW, generated file, NOT inlined as
   554	    # `environment: OPENAI_API_KEY: "${OPENAI_API_KEY:-}"`. That interpolation
   555	    # reads the *shell* env, and the app's provider keys do not live there: they
   556	    # live in the app database, configured in-app. So every normal install
   557	    # started this container with an EMPTY key, it logged "No LLM client
   558	    # configured - entity extraction will be limited", and — because extraction
   559	    # runs BEFORE the graph write — every episode was accepted and then dropped.
   560	    # The graph was silently empty. And an empty `environment:` value OVERRIDES
   561	    # an `env_file:` value on the same key (the nango/wayflow precedent above),
   562	    # so NO key this generator writes may appear in `environment:` below.
   563	    # `scripts/gen-graphiti-env.mjs` (run by `npm run services`) resolves the
   564	    # extraction provider AND the embedder choice from the app's stored provider
   565	    # configuration and writes docker/graphiti/.graphiti.env;
   566	    # `required: false` keeps a fresh checkout bootable, and a keyless bring-up
   567	    # is a reported state ("knowledge-graph indexing OFF"), not a silent one.
   568	    env_file:
   569	      - path: ./docker/graphiti/.graphiti.env
   570	        required: false
   571	    environment:
   572	      # The upstream server is YAML-first: CONFIG_PATH selects the file, and env
   573	      # vars (pydantic-settings, `__` nested delimiter) deep-merge OVER it. So
   574	      # this file holds the static shape and the generated env file holds the
   575	      # provider selection — the two never share a key.
   576	      CONFIG_PATH: /app/mcp/config/cinatra.yaml
   577	      # Database wiring is static and shares no key with the generated file.
   578	      DATABASE__PROVIDER: neo4j
   579	      DATABASE__PROVIDERS__NEO4J__URI: bolt://neo4j:7687
   580	      DATABASE__PROVIDERS__NEO4J__USERNAME: neo4j
   581	      DATABASE__PROVIDERS__NEO4J__PASSWORD: ${NEO4J_PASSWORD:-cinatra-local}
   582	      SEMAPHORE_LIMIT: "10"
   583	    volumes:
   584	      - ./docker/graphiti/config.yaml:/app/mcp/config/cinatra.yaml:ro
   585	    depends_on:
   586	      neo4j:
   587	        condition: service_healthy
   588	      kg-embedder:
   589	        condition: service_healthy
   590	    healthcheck:
   591	      # A REAL readiness route now (the old wrapper had none, which is why this
   592	      # was a bare TCP connect that went green before the server could serve).
   593	      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "curl -fsS http://localhost:8000/health || exit 1"]
   594	      interval: 10s
   595	      timeout: 5s
   596	      retries: 12
   597	      start_period: 20s

   599	  # WayFlow multi-tenant runtime.
   600	  # ONE container hosts every installed agent at /agents/<vendor>/<slug>/, mirroring
   601	  # the on-disk agents/<vendor>/<slug>/ tree. The loader (docker/wayflow/agent_loader.py)
   602	  # recursively scans agents/*/*/oas.json on startup and mounts each agent as an
   603	  # isolated A2AServer ASGI sub-application under a Starlette parent. No per-agent
   604	  # pinning env var; agent URL is derived from agent_templates.packageName.
   605	  #
   606	  # Profiles: [wayflow, drupal, wordpress] — the same shared runtime is brought up by
   607	  # any of the three operator commands; drupal/wordpress profiles still bring up their
   608	  # CMS containers (drupal/drupal-db, wordpress/wordpress-db) alongside.
   609	  #
   610	  # Start the runtime:    docker compose --profile wayflow up
   611	  # With Drupal CMS:      docker compose --profile drupal up
   612	  # With WordPress CMS:   docker compose --profile wordpress up
   613	  #
   614	  # Healthcheck probes GET /.health (returns {"status": "ok", "agents": <count>}).
   615	  # 12 retries × 10s gives the loader 2 minutes to mount all agents on cold start.
   616	  wayflow:
   617	    build:
   618	      context: ./docker/wayflow

   623	    # is sourced from a NARROW, generated file — NOT inlined as
   624	    # `environment: CINATRA_BRIDGE_TOKEN: "${CINATRA_BRIDGE_TOKEN}"`. That
   625	    # interpolation reads the *shell* env, so a bring-up that does not first
   626	    # `source .env.local` (a bare `docker compose up`, a CI harness) leaves the
   627	    # token EMPTY; worse, an empty `environment:` value OVERRIDES an `env_file`
   628	    # value on the same key, so the bridge silently 403s and the widget
   629	    # content-edit returns "(no response)". `scripts/gen-wayflow-env.mjs`
   630	    # (run by `npm run services`) writes docker/wayflow/.wayflow.env with ONLY
   631	    # those three keys; `required: false` keeps a fresh checkout bootable
   632	    # (the wayflow runtime then fails LOUD at boot if the bridge token is
   633	    # absent — agent_loader.py main()). Static, non-secret keys stay inline
   634	    # below: they are not interpolated, share no key with the env_file, and so
   635	    # never hit the empty-override trap (PORT especially must be 3010 here, NOT

   637	    env_file:

   645	      # No per-agent pinning env var is needed.
   646	    volumes:
   647	      - ./extensions:/agents:ro

   649	      - wayflow
   650	      - drupal

   648	export function listObjectsByFilter(
   649	  filter: ListObjectsFilter,
   650	  actor?: ActorContext,
   651	): ObjectRecord[] {
   652	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   653	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   654	
   655	  const where: string[] = [`(org_id = $1 OR $1 IS NULL)`, `deleted_at IS NULL`];
   656	  const values: unknown[] = [filter.orgId];
   657	  let pIdx = 2;
   658	
   659	  if (filter.type) {
   660	    where.push(`type = $${pIdx}`);
   661	    values.push(filter.type);
   662	    pIdx += 1;
   663	  }
   664	  if (filter.runId) {
   665	    where.push(`run_id = $${pIdx}`);
   666	    values.push(filter.runId);
   667	    pIdx += 1;
   668	  }
   669	  if (filter.ids && filter.ids.length > 0) {
   670	    where.push(`id = ANY($${pIdx}::text[])`);
   671	    values.push(filter.ids);
   672	    pIdx += 1;
   673	  }
   674	
   675	  // sealedRoomFilterValue() returns the effective projectId, or null when
   676	  // ambient or when the per-table feature flag is OFF. When non-null, append
   677	  // `AND project_id = $projectId` so the result is intersected with the
   678	  // project boundary. This clause runs before the per-actor ownership filter
   679	  // and after the `id IN (...)` clause, so a Graphiti candidate set from
   680	  // P+Q+ambient is filtered down to P-only. The decision lives here in the
   681	  // data layer; handlers can't bypass it.
   682	  const effectiveProjectId = sealedRoomFilterValue("objects", filter.projectId);
   683	  if (effectiveProjectId !== null) {
   684	    where.push(`project_id = $${pIdx}`);
   685	    values.push(effectiveProjectId);
   686	    pIdx += 1;
   687	  }
   688	
   689	  // cinatra#1456: indexed `data->>'<key>' = $n` correlation filters. Runs
   690	  // BEFORE the per-actor ownership filter so it intersects with org/type/
   691	  // sealed-room and cannot widen the read. Keys are allow-listed + pattern-
   692	  // validated in compileDataEqualsClause (the interpolated-key injection guard).
   693	  {
   694	    const { clauses, nextIndex } = compileDataEqualsClause(filter.dataEquals, values, pIdx);
   695	    for (const clause of clauses) where.push(clause);
   696	    pIdx = nextIndex;
   697	  }
   698	
   699	  // Splice buildOwnershipFilter into WHERE when actor present.
   700	  if (actor) {
   701	    const frag = buildOwnershipFilter(actor);
   702	    const remapped = frag.sql.replace(/\$(\d+)/g, (_m, n) => `$${Number(n) + pIdx - 1}`);
   703	    where.push(remapped);
   704	    values.push(...frag.params);
   705	    pIdx += frag.params.length;
   706	  }
   707	
   708	  const orderBy =
   709	    filter.ids && filter.ids.length > 0
   710	      ? "" // caller preserves Graphiti rank
   711	      : `ORDER BY created_at DESC`;
   712	  const limitClause = filter.limit
   713	    ? `LIMIT ${Math.min(filter.limit, 1000)}`
   714	    : "LIMIT 100";
   715	
   716	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   717	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   718	    queries: [
   719	      {
   720	        text: `SELECT id, type, parent_id, parent_type, data, created_at, updated_at,
   721	                 created_by, org_id, source, run_id, agent_id, package_version,
   722	                 agent_spec_version, version, deleted_at,
   723	                 owner_level, owner_id, visibility, project_id
   724	               FROM "${schema}"."objects"
   725	               WHERE ${where.join(" AND ")}
   726	               ${orderBy}
   727	               ${limitClause}`,
   728	        values,
   729	      },
   730	    ],
   731	  });
   732	
   733	  return (result?.rows ?? []).map(rowToObjectRecord);
   734	}
   735	
   736	/**
   737	 * Soft-delete: sets `deleted_at` and atomically appends a 'delete' outbox
   738	 * row. NEVER split into two `runPostgresQueriesSync` calls.
   739	 *
   740	 * Conditional outbox via CTE: the CTE below bumps `version`, flips
   741	 * `deleted_at`, and emits the outbox row in the same statement, only when a
   742	 * row actually transitioned. This preserves the atomic-outbox guarantee while
   743	 * eliminating spurious outbox rows for wrong-org or already-deleted updates.
   744	 */
   745	export function softDeleteObject(
   746	  id: string,
   747	  scope: { orgId: string | null },
   748	): { changeSetId: string | null } {
   749	  const { query } = buildSoftDeleteObjectQuery({ id, orgId: scope.orgId });
   750	  const [result] = runPostgresQueriesSync({
   751	    connectionString: getPostgresConnectionString(),
   752	    transaction: true,
   753	    queries: [query],
   754	  });
   755	  return { changeSetId: readSoftDeleteChangeSetId(result) };
   756	}
   757	
   758	/**
   759	 * Read the change_set id surfaced by a `buildSoftDeleteObjectQuery`
   760	 * statement result. NULL when the delete was a no-op (wrong org /
   761	 * already-deleted) — never an Undo deep-link to a change_set that was not
   762	 * created.
   763	 */
   764	export function readSoftDeleteChangeSetId(
   765	  result: { rows: Array<Record<string, unknown>> } | undefined,
   766	): string | null {
   767	  return (result?.rows?.[0]?.change_set_id as string | null | undefined) ?? null;
   768	}
   769	
   770	/**
   771	 * Build the CANONICAL soft-delete statement: one atomic CTE that flips
   772	 * `deleted_at`, bumps `version`, enqueues the Graphiti 'delete' outbox row,
   773	 * and appends the object_change_event + auto-closed restorable change_set
   774	 * (undo eligibility). This is the ONE soft-delete shape — `softDeleteObject`
   775	 * executes it standalone, and the artifact tombstone path (cinatra#1428
   776	 * deletion unification) composes it into the SAME transaction as its
   777	 * retention + provider-cache companions so an artifact delete can never
   778	 * bypass object history or the outbox again.
   779	 *
   780	 * The statement's final SELECT returns `change_set_id` (NULL on a no-op);
   781	 * read it with `readSoftDeleteChangeSetId`.
   782	 *
   783	 * `actorId`/`actorKind` attribute the change event + change_set; they
   784	 * default to the legacy system attribution when omitted.
   785	 */
   786	export function buildSoftDeleteObjectQuery(input: {
   787	  id: string;
   788	  orgId: string | null;
   789	  actorId?: string | null;
   790	  actorKind?: "user" | "agent" | "system";
   791	}): { query: { text: string; values: unknown[] }; changeSetId: string } {
   792	  assertWriteScopeAllowed("softDeleteObject", input.orgId);
   793	  ensurePostgresSchema();
   794	  const schema = postgresSchema.replaceAll('"', '""');
   795	
   796	  // Emit object_change_event in the same atomic CTE so legacy soft-delete
   797	  // callers participate in the data-safety history substrate.
   798	  const legacyChangeSetId = `cs_legacy_${randomUUID()}`;
   799	  const legacyEventId = randomUUID();
   800	  const legacyIdempotencyKey = `che_legacy_${randomUUID()}`;
   801	  const legacyChecksum = createHash("sha256")
   802	    .update(`legacy-writer:softDeleteObject:${input.id}:${legacyIdempotencyKey}`)
   803	    .digest("hex");
   804	
   805	  const query = {
   806	    text: `WITH base_row AS (
   807	                 SELECT version, row_to_json(o.*)::jsonb AS payload, type, org_id,
   808	                        project_id, owner_level, owner_id, visibility
   809	                 FROM "${schema}"."objects" o WHERE id = $1
   810	               ),
   811	               deleted AS (
   812	                 UPDATE "${schema}"."objects"
   813	                 SET deleted_at = now(),
   814	                     graphiti_sync_status = 'pending',
   815	                     version = COALESCE(version, 0) + 1,
   816	                     updated_at = now()
   817	                 WHERE id = $1
   818	                   AND (org_id = $2 OR $2 IS NULL)
   819	                   AND deleted_at IS NULL
   820	                 RETURNING id, version AS object_version, org_id, type, project_id,
   821	                           owner_level, owner_id, visibility,
   822	                           row_to_json("${schema}"."objects".*)::jsonb AS row_json
   823	               ),
   824	               outbox_row AS (
   825	                 INSERT INTO "${schema}"."graphiti_projection_outbox"
   826	                   (id, object_id, object_version, org_id, operation, payload_hash, status, attempts)
   827	                 SELECT gen_random_uuid()::text,
   828	                        deleted.id, deleted.object_version, deleted.org_id,
   829	                        'delete', NULL, 'pending', 0
   830	                 FROM deleted
   500	
   501	/**
   502	 * Ranked entity nodes -> canonical object ids, RANK PRESERVED.
   503	 *
   504	 * Walks the nodes in the order Graphiti ranked them and emits, per node, the
   505	 * deterministically-anchored row id when there is one, otherwise whatever the
   506	 * incidental probes can recover. De-duplicated, because two ranked nodes can
   507	 * legitimately resolve onto the same row (a merged anchor).
   508	 */
   509	function resolveObjectIds(nodes: EntityNode[], orgId: string | null): string[] {
   510	  const byAnchor = resolveObjectIdsByAnchorNodeUuids(
   511	    nodes.map((n) => n.uuid).filter((u): u is string => typeof u === "string"),
   512	    orgId,
   513	  );
   514	  const ordered: string[] = [];
   515	  const seen = new Set<string>();
   516	  for (const node of nodes) {
   517	    const deterministic = node.uuid ? byAnchor.get(node.uuid) : undefined;
   518	    const candidates = deterministic ? [deterministic] : extractIncidentalObjectIds([node]);
   519	    for (const id of candidates) {
   520	      if (!seen.has(id)) {
   521	        seen.add(id);
   522	        ordered.push(id);
   523	      }
   524	    }
   525	  }
   526	  return ordered;
   527	}

   530	// Authorization helpers
   531	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   532	//
   533	// `buildObjectResourceCheck` lifts the canonical owner_level / owner_id /
   534	// visibility off an ObjectRecord into the generic envelope consumed by
   535	// `enforceResourceAccess`. Objects do NOT have a co-owner table, so
  1290	      // meta.fallback="postgres_filter" + body from a Postgres-only list.
  1291	      // -----------------------------------------------------------------
  1292	      const groupId = resolveGroupId(orgId);
  1293	
  1294	      // Scope-recall lane entitlement (cinatra#1379 memory AC4, generalized to
  1295	      // artifact rows in cinatra#1436 AC3). LANE-ELIGIBLE rows — memory concepts
  1296	      // and artifact-scoped rows (the generic artifact type OR a claimed type
  1297	      // resolving to an artifact-safe / faceted disposition) — project into
  1298	      // NESTED per-scope lanes (user / team / ambient, optionally
  1299	      // project-suffixed), so a recall over the ambient org lane alone would
  1300	      // miss the caller's own user-lane and team-lane rows. For a lane-eligible
  1301	      // query we pass the actor's SERVER-DERIVED entitled lane set — own user
  1302	      // lane + a lane for every team the actor is a member of + the org lane,
  1303	      // each also in its `-proj-<id>` form when a projectId is in the call
  1304	      // context. A lane the actor is not entitled to is never in the set, so an
  1305	      // unentitled team's / project's rows can never surface. Every other type
  1306	      // keeps the single ambient org lane (unchanged). The lane set is relevance
  1307	      // scoping only: each candidate is still re-fetched + object.read-gated.
  1308	      let searchGroupIds: string[] = [groupId];
  1309	      const wantsEntitledLanes =
  1310	        input.type != null &&
  1311	        orgId != null &&
  1312	        (input.type === MEMORY_CONCEPT_TYPE_ID || isArtifactScopedRecallType(input.type));
  1313	      if (wantsEntitledLanes) {
  1314	        const actorUserId = actorExt.userId;
  1315	        let teamIds: string[] = [];
  1316	        if (actorUserId && orgId) {
  1317	          const teams = await readTeamsForUser(actorUserId, orgId);
  1318	          teamIds = teams.map((t) => t.id);
  1319	        }
  1320	        searchGroupIds = deriveEntitledLanes({
  1321	          orgId,
  1322	          userId: actorUserId,
  1323	          teamIds,
  1324	          projectId,
  1325	        });
  1326	      }
  1327	
  1328	      let objectIds: string[] | null = null;
  1329	      let degraded = false;
  1330	      try {
  1331	        const res = await searchNodes({
  1332	          query: input.query!,
  1333	          group_ids: searchGroupIds,
  1334	          max_nodes: input.limit ?? 50,
  1335	        });
  1336	        objectIds = resolveObjectIds(res.nodes, orgId);
  1337	      } catch (err) {
  1338	        console.warn(
  1339	          "[objects_list] searchNodes failed; falling back to Postgres-only filter:",
  1340	          err,
  1341	        );
  1342	        degraded = true;
  1343	      }
  1344	
  1345	      if (degraded || objectIds === null || objectIds.length === 0) {
  1346	        const rows = listObjectsByFilter(
  1347	          {
  1348	            orgId,
  1349	            type: input.type,
  1350	            runId: input.runId,
  1351	            dataEquals: input.dataEquals, // cinatra#1456 (Graphiti-fallback path)
  1352	            limit: input.limit,
  1353	            // Sealed-room filter applies on the Graphiti-fallback path too. The
  1354	            // user supplied a projectId; the result must stay inside the project
  1355	            // regardless of search path.
  1356	            projectId,
  1357	          },
  1358	          scopeActor,
  1359	        );
  1360	        const visible = await filterByAuthz(rows);
  1361	        const items = applyCategoryFilter(visible.map(mapRowToObject));
  1362	        // Distinguish "Graphiti unavailable" from "Graphiti responded, and
  1363	        // none of its ranked nodes named a row we hold".
  1364	        //
  1365	        // Since cinatra#2591 that second state means something much sharper

     1	# cinatra's configuration for the upstream Graphiti MCP server (cinatra#2591).
     2	#
     3	# HOW THIS FILE RELATES TO THE GENERATED ENV FILE
     4	# -----------------------------------------------
     5	# The upstream server layers its settings sources in this priority order:
     6	#
     7	#     CLI args  >  environment variables  >  THIS YAML  >  code defaults
     8	#
     9	# and the sources are DEEP-merged, not replaced. So the division of labour is:
    10	#
    11	#   * this file holds everything STATIC — transport, database wiring shape,
    12	#     entity/edge vocabulary. It is committed, readable, and diffable.
    13	#   * `docker/graphiti/.graphiti.env` (generated by scripts/gen-graphiti-env.mjs,
    14	#     0600, gitignored) holds everything that depends on what the OPERATOR
    15	#     configured in the app: which extraction provider, its key, which embedder,
    16	#     and the embedder's endpoint. Those keys override the placeholders here.
    17	#
    18	# NOTHING SECRET IS WRITTEN HERE. The provider blocks below carry no api_key at
    19	# all. A key only ever arrives through the generated env file, which is the one
    20	# artifact with 0600 permissions and a gitignore entry. If you find yourself
    21	# adding an `api_key:` line to this file, that is the bug.
    22	#
    23	# THE KEYLESS STATE IS DELIBERATE. With no generated env file (a fresh checkout,
    24	# or an install with no provider configured), the server starts with no LLM key
    25	# and extraction is off. That is a REPORTED state, not a silent one — the
    26	# bring-up says so, the app says so at boot, and the projection path says so
    27	# once per process. See src/lib/knowledge-graph-indexing.ts.
    28

   100	      description: "Information content in various forms (books, articles, reports, etc.)"
   101	    - name: "Topic"
   102	      description: "Subject of conversation, interest, or knowledge domain (use as last resort)"
   103	    - name: "Object"
   104	      description: "Physical items, tools, devices, or possessions (use as last resort)"
    55	# means something is wrong upstream and buffering it would be the failure.
    56	MAX_BODY_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("KG_EMBEDDER_MAX_BODY_BYTES", str(8 * 1024 * 1024)))
    57	
    58	_model: TextEmbedding | None = None
    59	_dimensions: int | None = None
    60	
    61	
    62	def model() -> TextEmbedding:
    63	    """The process-wide model. Loaded once, lazily, from the baked cache."""
    64	    global _model
    65	    if _model is None:
    66	        LOG.info("loading local embedding model %s", MODEL_NAME)
    67	        _model = TextEmbedding(model_name=MODEL_NAME)
    68	        LOG.info("local embedding model ready")
    69	    return _model
    70	
    71	
    72	def dimensions() -> int:
    73	    """Embedding width, measured from the model rather than hard-coded."""
    74	    global _dimensions
    75	    if _dimensions is None:
    76	        _dimensions = len(next(iter(model().embed(["dimension probe"]))))
    77	    return _dimensions
    78	
    79	
    80	def normalize_input(raw: Any) -> list[str]:
    81	    """OpenAI's `input` accepts a string or an array of strings.
    82	
    83	    Token-array inputs (the other shape the real API accepts) are REFUSED
    84	    rather than guessed at: graphiti only ever sends text, and silently
    85	    embedding a stringified list of integers would produce vectors that rank
    86	    nonsense instead of failing.
    87	    """
    88	    if isinstance(raw, str):
    89	        return [raw]
    90	    if isinstance(raw, list) and all(isinstance(item, str) for item in raw):
    91	        return list(raw)
    92	    raise ValueError("`input` must be a string or an array of strings")
    93	
    94	
    95	def embed(texts: list[str]) -> list[list[float]]:
    96	    return [list(map(float, vector)) for vector in model().embed(texts)]
    97	
    98	
    99	class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
   100	    protocol_version = "HTTP/1.1"
   101	    server_version = "cinatra-kg-embedder"
   102	
   103	    def log_message(self, fmt: str, *args: Any) -> None:  # noqa: A003 - stdlib hook
   104	        LOG.info("%s %s", self.address_string(), fmt % args)
   105	
   106	    def _send(self, status: int, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   107	        body = json.dumps(payload).encode("utf-8")
   108	        self.send_response(status)
   109	        self.send_header("Content-Type", "application/json")
   110	        self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
   111	        self.end_headers()
   112	        self.wfile.write(body)
   113	
   114	    def _error(self, status: int, message: str) -> None:
   115	        # OpenAI's error envelope — the SDK on the other side reads `error.message`.
   116	        self._send(status, {"error": {"message": message, "type": "invalid_request_error"}})
   117	
   118	    def do_GET(self) -> None:  # noqa: N802 - stdlib hook
   119	        if self.path.rstrip("/") in ("/health", ""):
   120	            self._send(200, {"status": "ok", "model": MODEL_NAME, "dimensions": dimensions()})
   121	            return
   122	        self._error(404, f"no route {self.path}")
   123	
   124	    def do_POST(self) -> None:  # noqa: N802 - stdlib hook
   125	        if self.path.rstrip("/") != "/v1/embeddings":
   126	            self._error(404, f"no route {self.path}")
   127	            return
   128	
   129	        try:
   130	            length = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
   131	        except ValueError:
   132	            self._error(400, "Content-Length is not a number")
   133	            return
   134	        if length <= 0:
   135	            self._error(400, "empty request body")
   136	            return
   137	        if length > MAX_BODY_BYTES:
   138	            self._error(413, f"request body exceeds {MAX_BODY_BYTES} bytes")
   139	            return
   140	
   141	        try:
   142	            payload = json.loads(self.rfile.read(length))
   143	            texts = normalize_input(payload.get("input"))
   144	        except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError) as exc:
   145	            self._error(400, str(exc))
   146	            return
   147	
   148	        if not texts:
   149	            self._error(400, "`input` must contain at least one string")
   150	            return
   151	
   152	        try:
   153	            vectors = embed(texts)
   154	        except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
   155	            LOG.exception("embedding failed")
   156	            self._error(500, f"embedding failed: {exc}")
   157	            return
   158	
   159	        self._send(
   160	            200,
   161	            {
   162	                "object": "list",
   163	                # Echo the requested model name so a caller that asserts on it
   164	                # sees its own value; `model_local` states what actually ran.
   165	                "model": payload.get("model") or MODEL_NAME,
   166	                "model_local": MODEL_NAME,
   167	                "data": [
   168	                    {"object": "embedding", "index": i, "embedding": v}
   169	                    for i, v in enumerate(vectors)
   170	                ],
   171	                # The real API bills tokens; there is nothing to bill here and a
   172	                # fabricated count would be a lie in a ledger, so both are zero.
   173	                "usage": {"prompt_tokens": 0, "total_tokens": 0},
   174	            },
   175	        )
   176	
   177	
   178	def main() -> None:
   179	    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s %(message)s")
   180	    # Load before binding, so readiness means "can actually embed" rather than
   181	    # "the port is open" — the exact distinction the old graphiti healthcheck
   182	    # could not make.
   183	    LOG.info("local embedder: model=%s dimensions=%d", MODEL_NAME, dimensions())
   184	    server = ThreadingHTTPServer((HOST, PORT), Handler)
   185	    LOG.info("local embedder listening on %s:%d", HOST, PORT)
   186	    server.serve_forever()
   187	
   188	
   189	if __name__ == "__main__":
   190	    main()
     1	# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
     2	#
     3	# cinatra local embedder — the vendor-free embedding floor (cinatra#2591).
     4	# See server.py for WHY this exists; this file is only how it is packaged.
     5	#
     6	# fastembed runs sentence-transformers models through ONNX Runtime, so the image
     7	# needs no torch (which would add ~2.5 GB). The model weights are pulled at BUILD
     8	# time into the image's cache directory, so a container start needs no network
     9	# and no Hugging Face reachability — a local floor that silently required an
    10	# internet round trip on every cold start would not be a floor.
    11	
    12	FROM python:3.12-slim-bookworm
    13	
    14	ARG KG_EMBEDDER_MODEL=BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5
    15	
    16	RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
    17	      curl ca-certificates \
    18	    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
    19	
    20	# FASTEMBED_CACHE_PATH is set explicitly because that is what lets the build
    21	# bake the weights into a layer instead of downloading them at container start.
    22	# (A comment cannot sit inside a line continuation, so it lives here.)
    23	ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    24	    KG_EMBEDDER_MODEL=${KG_EMBEDDER_MODEL} \
    25	    KG_EMBEDDER_PORT=8080 \
    26	    FASTEMBED_CACHE_PATH=/opt/kg-embedder/models \
    27	    HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1
    28	
    29	WORKDIR /app
    30	
    31	RUN pip install --no-cache-dir "fastembed==0.8.0"
    32	
    33	COPY server.py /app/server.py
    34	
    35	# Bake the weights. This also fails the BUILD (not the first request) if the
    36	# model name is wrong or the download is incomplete.
    37	RUN python -c "from fastembed import TextEmbedding; import os; m = TextEmbedding(model_name=os.environ['KG_EMBEDDER_MODEL']); print('dimensions', len(next(iter(m.embed(['bake probe'])))))"
    38	
    39	EXPOSE 8080
    40	
    41	HEALTHCHECK --interval=10s --timeout=5s --start-period=20s --retries=12 \
    42	    CMD curl -fsS http://localhost:8080/health || exit 1
    43	
    44	CMD ["python", "/app/server.py"]

   220	//   unknown     → emit. "Unknown" means the question could not be answered (no
   221	//                 probe bound, an unreadable configuration), and the defect this
   222	//                 metering exists to fix is spend that is INVISIBLE — an
   223	//                 unattributed row beats a dropped one.
   224	//
   225	// NOTE the vocabulary: `configured` is what the APP holds, not proof of what
   226	// the running container was started with (the pinned wrapper reports no
   227	// readiness). A key saved after the container started is configured and not yet
   228	// in use, which is why nothing here claims "indexing is on".
   229	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   230	
   231	export type KnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState = {
   232	  providerKey: "configured" | "absent" | "unknown";
   233	  /** Operator-facing explanation. NEVER carries a key or any part of one. */
   234	  reason: string;
   235	};
   236	
   237	const UNKNOWN_PROVIDER_KEY_STATE: KnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState = {
   238	  providerKey: "unknown",
   239	  reason: "no knowledge-graph indexing probe is bound in this process",
   240	};
   241	
   242	let indexingProbe: (() => KnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState) | null = null;
   243	let warnedIndexingOff = false;
   244	
   245	/**
   246	 * Bind the host's indexing-state probe. Idempotent; the host calls it at boot.
   247	 * The probe must be cheap (the host caches its own read) and must never return
   248	 * the key itself.
   249	 */
   250	export function setKnowledgeGraphIndexingProbe(
   251	  probe: (() => KnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState) | null,
   252	): void {
   253	  indexingProbe = probe;
   254	}
   255	
   256	/** Resolve the current state. A throwing probe degrades to `unknown`. */
   257	export function readKnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState(): KnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState {
   258	  if (!indexingProbe) return UNKNOWN_PROVIDER_KEY_STATE;
   259	  try {
   260	    return indexingProbe();
   261	  } catch (err) {
   262	    return {
   263	      providerKey: "unknown",
   264	      reason: `provider-key probe failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`,
   265	    };
   266	  }
   267	}
   268	
   269	/** Test seam: the once-per-process warning latch. */
   270	export function __resetKnowledgeGraphIndexingWarningForTests(): void {
   271	  warnedIndexingOff = false;
   272	}
   273	
   274	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   275	// Public API
   276	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   277	
   278	export async function addEpisode(input: AddEpisodeInput): Promise<AddEpisodeResult> {
   279	  // Tool is named "add_memory" in knowledge-graph-mcp 1.0.x (not "add_episode")
   280	  const raw = await callMcp("add_memory", input as Record<string, unknown>);
   281	  // Metered on the CALL succeeding, not on the acknowledgement parsing. The
   282	  // hand-over is what causes the provider fan-out; an acknowledgement whose
   283	  // SHAPE we fail to recognize is still an episode Graphiti accepted and billed,
   284	  // and dropping its row would put the spend back where this change found it.
   285	  recordEpisodeUsage();
   286	  return addEpisodeResultSchema.parse(raw);
   287	}
   288	
   289	/**
   290	 * Put ONE `usage_events` row on the bus per episode actually handed over
   291	 * (cinatra#2582 / the Graphiti line item of cinatra#2578).
   292	 *
   293	 * Emitted after the call returns, so a failed hand-over never books spend.
   294	 * The row is counted and UNPRICED — see `GraphitiUsageEvent` for why inventing
   295	 * a token count would be worse than admitting we do not have one.
   296	 *
   297	 * Never throws: metering must not break the projection path (the bus emitter
   298	 * swallows too, this is the second belt).
   299	 */
   300	function recordEpisodeUsage(): void {
   301	  try {
   302	    const state = readKnowledgeGraphProviderKeyState();
   303	    if (state.providerKey === "absent") {
   304	      if (!warnedIndexingOff) {
   305	        warnedIndexingOff = true;
   306	        console.warn(
   307	          "[graphiti] knowledge-graph indexing is OFF — no provider key in the app's stored " +
   308	            `configuration (${state.reason}). Episodes are accepted by the indexer and then ` +
   309	            "dropped: extraction runs before the graph write, so the graph stays empty. " +
   310	            "Configure an OpenAI provider key, then re-run the bring-up so the indexer " +
   311	            "container picks it up.",
   312	        );
   313	      }
   314	      return;
   315	    }
   316	    emitUsageEvent({
   317	      source: "graphiti",
   318	      provider: "openai",
   319	      operation: "episode",
   320	      model: null,
   321	      idempotencyKey: `graphiti:episode:${randomUUID()}`,
   322	      occurredAt: new Date().toISOString(),
   323	    });
   324	  } catch (err) {
   325	    console.warn("[graphiti] usage metering failed (episode still sent):", err);
   420	  case "$(printf '%s' "$SEED_ANSWER" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')" in
   421	    y|yes) LOAD_SEED=1 ;;
   422	    *)     LOAD_SEED=0 ;;
   423	  esac
   424	fi
   425	
   426	if [ "$LOAD_SEED" = "1" ]; then
   427	  info "Loading sample fixture data (ACME Group)..."
   428	  pnpm seed || warn "Sample data load reported an issue. Re-run with: pnpm seed"
   429	fi

   431	# ── Knowledge-graph provider key (cinatra#2582) ───────────────────────────────
   432	
   433	# The Graphiti container needs an OpenAI key as a static env var at container
   434	# startup, and the app keeps that key in the DATABASE, not the shell env. Compose
   435	# used to interpolate `${OPENAI_API_KEY:-}`, which resolved EMPTY on a normal

   858	    },

   861	  const misses = sampleIds.filter(
   862	    (id) => !episodes.some((e) => e.name.includes(`[oid:${id}]`) || e.content.includes(id)),
   863	  );
   864	  if (misses.length > 0) {

   303	      return /projected_group_id\s*=\s*NULL/.test(t) && /graphiti_episode_uuid\s*=\s*NULL/.test(t);
   304	    });
   305	    expect(retractCall).toBeDefined();
   306	  });

   308	  it("a NEVER-projected public row just skips (no delete, no retract UPDATE)", async () => {

   310	    const result = await projectObjectToGraphiti({ objectId: "obj-mem-1", objectVersion: 1, orgId: ORG });
   311	    expect(result.skipped).toBe(true);
   312	    expect(delEp).not.toHaveBeenCalled();

   314	    expect(retractCall).toBeUndefined();
   315	  });

   317	  it("a retract delete failure is BEST-EFFORT — still clears bookkeeping and skips (recall authz is Postgres-side)", async () => {
   318	    const priorLane = `${BASE}-user-user-42`;
   319	    delEp.mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error("delete flaked on retract"));

   321	      rows: [memoryRow({ version: 2, graphiti_projected_version: 1, projected_group_id: priorLane, visibility: "public" })],
   322	    }]);

   324	    const result = await projectObjectToGraphiti({ objectId: "obj-mem-1", objectVersion: 2, orgId: ORG });
   325	    expect(result.skipped).toBe(true);
   326	    expect(addEp).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
   327	    // Bookkeeping is still retracted so a re-enqueue does not loop on the purge.

   329	    expect(retractCall).toBeDefined();
